Derived algebras on formal stacks and prismatic gauges
Shubhankar Sahai

Abstract
. This paper studies how the theory of derived algebras (in the sense of Bhatt-Mathew and Raksit) interacts with formal derived geometry, specifically the formal derived stacks which show up in the theory of prismatization. As an application we prove some classification theorems for derived algebras in quasi-coherent sheaves on a certain class of filtered formal stacks, which includes those whose quasi-coherent sheaves are prismatic gauges over a perfectoid ring. Along the way, among other things, we study the behavior of derived algebras along schematic quasi-affine morphisms in derived geometry, and for example, classify derived algebras on the source as precisely those derived algebras on the target which receive a map from the pushforward of the structure sheaf of the source. We also indicate how to extend some of our results to (formal) classifying stacks of diagonalizable group schemes. As an aside, we also show some classification theorems even for quasi-coherent sheaves on formal stacks which (to our knowledge) weren’t available in the literature on derived geometry previously. These results are motivated by forthcoming work of the author [ Sah26a ] and [ Sah26b ] but are hoped to be generally useful.

Primary
14\text{A}30
; Secondary
14\text{F}08

1.
Introduction

The paper grew out of an attempt to understand the interaction of derived algebras of Bhatt-Mathew and Raksit
[
Rak26
]
, which serve as a robust notion of non-connective animated (equivalently simplicial commutative) rings in many settings, with derived completions and derived formal geometry. As a consequence, in this note we develop some basic results on non-connective formal geometry which seem to not be in the literature.

Another motivation was to extend the classification, due to Simpson
[
Sim97
]
, of the filtered derived category over a discrete ring as quasi-coherent sheaves on
\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}
, to derived algebras in the completed setting, following the modern techniques adopted by Moulinos
[
Mou21
]
. As a consequence of this second motivation, we use the techniques developed in this note to classify derived algebras in quasi-coherent sheaves on a certain class of filtered formal stacks which live over
B\mathbf{G}_{m}
and factor over the canonical projection
\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}.

The overarching motivation for proving these results stems from forthcoming work of the author
[
Sah26a
]
and
[
Sah26b
]
, and are already used in
[
Sah25
]
.

While we explain these works at some length in §
5
, we are hopeful that the results presented herein will be more widely applicable. As an example, some of our results in §
2
and §
4.2
and already seem useful in developing a Tannakian framework (in the sense of
[
Lur11b
]
) in the formal derived setting, which is the subject of ongoing investigations
[
MS
]
(for example see Remark
2.34
).

We will begin with some extended background in §
1.1
to contextualize the technical nature of our results. An explanation of our results themselves is in §
1.2
.

1.1.
Background.

The notion of animated or simplicial commutative rings, introduced systematically in the pioneering work of Quillen
[
Qui70a
]
and
[
Qui70b
]
, is now an essential tool in (derived) algebraic geometry. Indeed, the theory is now quite mature and standard; for example, see
[
BO21
]
for some beautiful recent applications to questions of representability of flat cohomology.

The difference between an animated ring and a general connective
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring is that the latter, in positive or mixed characteristic, will not be equivalent to a simplicial commutative ring. In other words, the multiplication on a general connective
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring is not
strict
(for a precise modern explanation of this notion of strictness, see
[
Lur18
, § 25.1.5]
).

While animated rings are sufficient for many purposes, in (even classical) algebraic geometry one has to often work with
coconnective

\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-rings which are not animated rings, but admit a notion of strictification in the sense that they can be modeled (along with the multiplication) by cosimplicial commutative rings.

Example 1.1
.

Let
k
be a perfect field of positive characteristic and let
X/k
be an ordinary
K3
-surface i.e. the Frobenius endomorphism
F:H^{2}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\to H^{2}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
is an isomorphism.

The coconnective
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-algebra given by the derived ring of global sections
\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
then can be computed by a Cech complex giving rise to a cosimplicial model for its multiplication.

This cosimplicial ring is not formal in the sense that it is not isomorphic to the graded ring
H^{0}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\oplus H^{2}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})[-2]
as the following argument we learned from S. Petrov
1
1

1

All mistakes are, of course, our own.
shows. The non-formality can be checked at the level of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-rings and one notes that the power operation
P^{0}\colon H^{i}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\to H^{i}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
of
[
Lur11d
, Remark 2.27]
, which corresponds to the Frobenius endomorphism of
\Gamma(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
by
[
Pet25
, Proposition 1.6]
, is not zero for
i=2
.

As Example
1.1
shows, even in classical algebraic geometry one has to work with non-formal cosimplicial rings. This theory has a very different feel from the theory of animated rings. For example if
A
is an animated ring, then
A
can be thought of as a pro-nilpotent thickening of
\pi_{0}(A)
. There is no natural substitute for this deformation theoretic interpretation in the cosimplicial world (at least to the author’s knowledge). Another aspect, already implicit in Example
1.1
, is that cosimplicial commutative rings carry a Frobenius, while animated rings do not
[
BS17
, Proposition 11.6]
.

The theory of derived rings invented by Bhatt-Mathew, Raksit
[
Rak26
]
and Brantner-Mathew
[
BM25
]
provides a robust theory of ‘nonconnective’ animated rings, gluing both the simplicial and cosimplicial degrees, in quite general contexts akin to commutative algebra objects in
\infty\text{-categories}
; for example one has a notion of derived algebras in quasi-coherent sheaves on (classical) algebraic stacks
[
KP24
, Appendix A.2]
. We briefly recall the theory now.

Recollection 1.2

(Classical monadicity of rings)
.

Let
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\heartsuit}
denote the abelian category of discrete
\mathbf{Z}
-modules and let
\mathrm{Rings}
be the
1
-category of discrete rings. Then the forgetful functor
U\colon\mathrm{Rings}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\heartsuit}
admits a left adjoint
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}(-):=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\mathrm{Sym}^{i}_{\mathbf{Z}}(-).

The composite

U\circ\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\heartsuit}

is a monad wherein the multiplication is induced by the natural map
\mathrm{Sym}^{i}_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{j}(M))\to\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{i.j}(M)
, where
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\heartsuit}.

It is not too hard to show that this adjunction is monadic by Barr-Beck, whence it induces an equivalence of
1
-categories

\mathrm{Rings}\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{U\circ\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{*}}(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\heartsuit}).

The monadicity theorem above admits a robust generalization to the animated setting with
almost the same proof
, but with Barr-Beck replaced by Barr-Beck-Lurie.

Recollection 1.3

(Monadicity for animated rings)
.

Now let
\mathrm{ARing}
be the
\infty\text{-category}
of animated (or simplicial commutative) rings and
(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{\geq 0}
the
\infty\text{-category}
of connective
\mathbf{Z}
-modules. There is an obvious conservative and limit preserving forgetful functor
U\colon\mathrm{ARing}\to(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{\geq 0}
. By the adjoint functor theorem for presentable
\infty\text{-categories}
, there exists a left adjoint
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}\colon(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{\geq 0}\to\mathrm{ARing}
and can be described explicitly as

\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{n}_{\mathbf{Z}}(-),

where
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{n}_{\mathbf{Z}}
, the summand in the
n
th degree on the right hand side, is the
animation
of the classical
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{n}
functor of Recollection
1.2
.
It again follows by a simple application of the
\infty
-categorical Barr-Beck (a.k.a Barr-Beck-Lurie) that the forgetful functor
U
is monadic and exhibits an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{ARing}\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}}((\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{\geq 0})

where by abuse of notation we identify
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}
with the composite
U\circ\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}.

Note that
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}
in Recollection
1.3
uses the functors
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}
on the connective part of
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
, by animating (or left Kan extending) the values of
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}
on finite free (hence discrete)
\mathbf{Z}
-modules. If one wants to incorporate the notion of global sections as in Example
1.1
as well as animated rings into an ambient category of possible non-connective animated rings, then
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}
as described above doesn’t work.

However the idea going back to Illusie
[
Ill71
, § I.4]
is that if one can appropriately define a monad

\widetilde{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}},

whose values extend those of
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}
on the connective part and the right Kan extension of
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}
on the coconnective part, then one would have a robust notion of non-connective animated rings.

The endofunctors
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{n}_{\mathbf{Z}}
for
n>0
were extended on
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
by using a notion of
right-left
extensions of functors by Brantner-Mathew
[
BM25
, § 3]
, and the monad
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}
was extended as a
filtered monad
to the full category by Raksit in
[
Rak26
, § 4]
.

Thereafter, one defines the
\infty
-category of
derived rings
as

\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}:=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}}(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})

where, by abuse of notation, we also denote the extension
\widetilde{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}}
of
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbf{Z}}
to the unbounded category by the same notation.

This theory has several deep applications and we refer the reader to
[
Ant25
]
,
[
MM24
]
,
[
MR26
]
,
[
Pet25
]
and
[
Rak26
]
among others.

In
[
Rak26
, Definition 4.2.1]
, the author defines a notion of
derived algebraic contexts
which, in the
p
-local case, are
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}
-linear objects in
\Pr^{L}
, the
\infty
-category of presentable
\infty
-categories, satisfying some additional conditions like being symmetric monoidal, having a right complete
t
-structure which is compatible with filtered colimits, the connective part being compactly generated by a subcategory of the heart, etc and shows that you can derive the filtered monad
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}
on a derived algebraic context and you get a good working theory of non-connective animated rings in that context.

Definition 1.4
.

Let
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
denote the
\infty\text{-category}
of
p
-complete
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
- modules. An
\infty\text{-category}

\mathcal{C}\in\Pr^{L}
is called
p
-complete if it admits a structure of a
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
-module in
\Pr^{L}.
2
2

2

This terse definition is a consequence of the idempotence of
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
as a
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}
-algebra in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}}(\Pr^{L}
). In other words, membership in
(\Pr^{L})^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is a property not a structure. See
[
AKN23
, Appendix A]
.
We write
(\Pr^{L})^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
for the
\infty
-category of
p
-complete presentable
\infty\text{-categories}
.

We now give an example of how
p
-complete
\infty\text{-categories}
arise in nature.

Example 1.5

(
p
-complete
\infty\text{-categories}
arise as categories of quasi-coherent sheaves on stacks.)
.

Let
X
be a stack i.e. an fpqc sheaf of spaces on
\mathrm{ARing}.
We will say a stack is a
p
-adic formal stack if the structure morphism
X\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{Z})
factors over
\operatorname{Spf}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{Z}).
This induces a canonical action of
\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}))=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}^{{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
on
\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
, whence
\mathrm{QCoh}(X)\in(\Pr^{L})^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

One of our underlying goals in this paper is to understand how the the theory of derived rings interacts with objects in
(\Pr^{L})^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
which arise as above.

However one hits an obstruction even when the formal stack is affine, as shown by the example of
\operatorname{Spf}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
, the final object in the
\infty\text{-category}
of
p
-adic formal stacks.
Indeed,
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}=\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}))
, the unit in
(\Pr^{L})^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is not a derived algebraic context as its
t
-structure is not compatible with filtered colimits in the sense of
[
Lur17
, Definition 1.3.5.20]
i.e. the coconnective part is not stable under filtered colimits.

Example 1.6
.

The
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is pulled back along the inclusion
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}
i.e. a module is (co)connective if it is as a
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-module. We will show that colimits of coconnective modules can become non-trivially connective.
Recall that colimits in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
are computed in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}
and completed. By considering the cofiber

\mathbf{Z}_{p}\to\mathbf{Q}_{p}\to\mathbf{Q}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}_{p}

we see that
(\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}\mathbf{Z}/p^{n})^{\wedge}_{p}=(\mathbf{Q}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}_{p})^{\wedge}_{p}=\mathbf{Z}_{p}[1]
which is not coconnective.
3
3

3

One can try to repair this by working with the
torsion

t
-structure. We do not know if this gives an equivalent notion.

While Example
1.6
shows that it is not possible to intrinsically derive the
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}
monad on
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}^{{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
in the sense of Raksit, there are many instances in nature where
p
-complete derived algebras show up.

Example 1.7
.

Let
X/\mathbf{F}_{p}
be a smooth affine scheme. Then its crystalline cohomology
\Gamma_{\mathrm{cris}}(X/\mathbf{Z}_{p})
is a
p
-complete derived algebra in the sense that its underlying module lives in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}^{{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
. The fact that it is a derived algebra can be seen because the cohomology can be computed using
p
-complete cosimplicial rings given by Cech-Alexander complex. See
[
BdJ11
, § 2]
4
4

4

Note that by classical deformation theory, one may lift such a smooth affine scheme all the way to
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
, whence one may also recover the derived algebra structure by contemplating the
p
-complete de Rham complex of the lift, for example by the main result of
loc.cit
.
.

Similar structures show up routinely in the theory of prismatic cohomology
[
BS22
]
, which can be seen as a mixed characteristic lift of crystalline cohomology. Moreover, in the the theory of prismatization
[
Dri24
]
,
[
BL22a
]
,
[
BL22b
]
and
[
Bha22
]
one has to contemplate not just individual
p
-complete derived algebras but in fact entire
p
-complete (in the sense of Definiton
1.4
) categories of coefficients.

Example 1.8
.

Let
X/\mathbf{Z}_{p}
be a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme. Then there is a functorial assignment of a
p
-adic formal stack
X\mapsto X^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
so that

\Gamma(X^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}},{\mathcal{O}}_{X^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})\simeq\Gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(X)

where the right hand side is the absolute prismatic cohomology of
X.
In this setting,
\mathrm{QCoh}(X^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})\in(\Pr^{L})^{p\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
and can be regarded as a category of coefficients for prismatic cohomology in the following sense: If
f\colon Y\to X
is a smooth proper morphism then
f^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\in\mathrm{Perf}(X^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
carries information about the absolute prismatic cohomology of
Y
and, by derived base change, of the fibers of
f
.

The functor
f^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{*}
carries derived algebras in quasi-coherent sheaves on
Y
to derived algebras on
X
. In particular
f^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
is a derived algebra on
X.
We will show in
[
Sah26b
]
that it is in fact fruitful to take this refined multiplicative structure on
f^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
seriously.

The
p
-adic formal stacks above are often formal not just in the
p
-adic direction, but also the
prismatic direction
.
5
5

5

A perhaps simplistic viewpoint of the prismatic theory which is heuristically implicit in this note is the following. A prism is a pair
(A,I)
of a (discrete) ring
A
and an ideal
I
with
I\in\mathrm{Pic}(A)
. One requires that
A
be derived
(p,I)
-complete and treats the
I
-direction as a formal parameter, whence
A
may be treated as a pro-nilpotent thickening of the
p
-complete ring
A/I
. Further one requires that
A
carry a derived lift of the Frobenius on
A/p
, encapsulated in the notion of a
\delta
-ring.
The theory then provides a deformation of the (derived) de Rham cohomology functor
\mathrm{dR}_{-/(A/I)}
to a commutative algebra
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{-/A}
over
A
along with a Frobenius coming from the
\delta
-structure. It is therefore this
I
-direction we treat as formal.
Indeed this happens when the structure morphism
X\to\operatorname{Spf}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
factors over
\operatorname{Spf}({\mathcal{O}}_{C})
, the ring of integers in a completed algebraic closure of
C
of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
. This adds some complexity to their categories of quasi-coherent sheaves which can now be complete for two parameters (in this case the kernel of the canonical surjection
A_{\mathrm{inf}}({\mathcal{O}}_{C})\to{\mathcal{O}}_{C}
, where the source is Fontaine’s famous period ring) and not just
p
-complete.

Motivated by the examples above, our main goal now is to explain how the theory of derived algebras interacts with the formal completions in derived algebraic geometry.

1.2.
Results.

.

To make the idea precise we fix some notation.

Notation 1.9
.

For this subsection and the next, fix an animated ring
A
and a finitely generated ideal
J\subset\pi_{0}(A).
Let
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
be the
\infty\text{-category}
of
J
-complete
A
-modules and let
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
denote the
\infty
-category of derived
A
-algebras, defined as
(\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{A/}.

In the sequel we will study how the notion of derived algebras interacts with
\infty\text{-categories}
of quasi-coherent sheaves on geometric objects over
A
which are
J
-complete.

1.2.1.
J
-complete derived algebras.

Our first results correspond to the notion of
J
-complete derived algebras. See §
2
for more on the following thorem.

Theorem 1.10
.

There is a localizing subcategory
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
of
J
-complete derived algebras with the following properties

(1)

\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is a presentably symmetric monoidal
\infty\text{-category}
.

(2)

The left adjoint is completion at the level of underlying modules.

(3)

The forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is monadic with left adjoint
(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A})^{\wedge}_{J}
, the
J
-adic completion of the monad
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A}.

(4)

The forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is symmetric monoidal, conservative and preserves all (co)limits.

(5)

When one right Kan extends
\mathrm{DAlg}(-)
on all prestacks, then
\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))=\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
.

(6)

The assignment
B\mapsto\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{B}
on
A
-algebras is local for the
J
-completely flat topology.

To prove this we also give a proof of a folklore theorem which seems to not be available in the literature. Note that there is no completeness conditions on
A
, although the statement of course depends only on the
J
-completion of
A.

Theorem 1.11
.

There is a canonical equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

See Remark
2.25
for some literature concerning the above theorem as far as the author is aware.

1.2.2.
Graded derived algebras and comonads.

In §
3
we prove several comonadicity theorems for graded derived algebras over
A
, in both the
J
-complete and noncomplete setting. The results are generically of the following form (see Corollary
3.31
).

Theorem 1.12
.

Let
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
be the
\infty\text{-category}
of graded derived algebras such that each graded piece is
J
-complete and let
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
be the
\infty\text{-category}
described in the previous subsection. Then there’s a ‘continuous’ comonadic adjunction

(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\colon\pi^{*}

and the ‘continuous’ comonad
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\circ\pi^{*}
can be identified with a ‘continuous’ action of the completed bicommutative Hopf algebra
(-)\widehat{\otimes}_{A}A[\mathbf{Z}].

Here the functor
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is a completed version of the compactly supported pushforward of local systems of spectra which shows up in the theory of parametrised spectra
[
ABG18
]
.

In Remarks
3.17
and
3.32
we explain how to generalize mutatis mutandis the result above to the case of a continuous coaction by the bicommutative Hopf algebra
\mathbf{Z}[M]
where
M
is an arbitrary abelian group.

1.2.3.
Derived algebras on geometric stacks.

In this section we study the geometric theory of derived algebras. We give a sampling of the results proved in this section.

One of the first results we prove is the following

Theorem 1.13
.

For
A
as in Notation
1.9
, there is a canonical equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))\simeq\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}

where
\operatorname{Spf}(A)
is taken in the
J
-adic direction.

Note that Theorem
1.13
is not a formal consequence of the easier statement for
\mathrm{QCoh}
. See Remark
4.54
. Thus this is proved using the continuous comonadicity established in Theorem
1.12
.

In Remark
4.57
, we will explain how to prove Theorem
1.13
in the case one replaces
B\mathbf{G}_{m}
with the classifying stack
B\mathbf{D}(M)
where
\mathbf{D}(M)
is the diagonalizable group scheme associated to an arbitrary abelian group
M
, e.g. as explained in
[
Tat97
, § 2]
.

We will use this result along with the notion of quasi-affine morphisms in derived algebraic geometry, which we now recall.

Following
[
GR17
]
we will say that a morphism
f\colon X\to Y
of derived stacks is quasi-affine if for any animated ring
R
with a morphism
{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)\to Y
, the base change
X\times_{Y}{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
is representable by a quasi-compact quasi-affine derived scheme (the latter condition meaning that it admits an open immersion into an affine scheme). Then our result (see Proposition
4.21
) is the following:

Theorem 1.14
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a quasi-affine morphism of derived stacks. Then there is an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)_{f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}/}.

Similar techniques which go in the proof of Theorem
1.15
, also allow us to deduce a (derived) base change theorem for derived algebras in Proposition
4.23
.

We apply these result to quasi-affine stacks over
B\mathbf{G}_{m}
to obtain results of the following form. Recall that a prism
(A,I)
is called orientable if the effective Cartier divisor
I\in\mathrm{Pic}(A)
admits a global generator
d\in I
. See Example
4.75
.

Theorem 1.15
.

Let
(A,I)
be an orientable bounded prism. Fix an orientation
d\in I
. Then there’s an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}^{(p,d)\mathrm{-comp}}_{\mathrm{Fil}_{(d)}A}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A[u,t]/(ut-d))/\mathbf{G}_{m})

where the formal direction on the right is
(p,d)
-adic.

Note that when
(A,I)
is a perfect prism, then there’s a canonical isomorphism

(A/I)^{\mathrm{Nyg}}\simeq\operatorname{Spf}(A[u,t]/(ut-d))/\mathbf{G}_{m}

and since
\mathrm{DAlg}
is functorial in stacks we have

Corollary 1.16
.

There is an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}^{(p,d)\mathrm{-comp}}_{\mathrm{Fil}_{(d)}A}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}((A/I)^{\mathrm{Nyg}})

In
[
Sah26a
]
, for any bounded prism
(A,I)
we will define the relative Nygaard filtered prismatization following the constructions implicit in
[
Bha22
, § 5]
. We will show that the
relative Nygaard filtered prismatization provides a good theory of coefficients for Nygaard filtered prismatic cohomology. Theorem
1.15
then plays the main role therein.

Remark 1.17
.

From our perspective, the key issues solved in this note are how to work around the non-obvious functoriality of the notion of
\mathrm{DAlg}
. As an example of what would be desirable, see Remark
2.17
.

1.3.
Forthcoming work.

The tools developed in this note are in service to the the authors’ forthcoming work
[
Sah26a
]
and
[
Sah26b
]
. We will explain the relation of our current paper with the ones cited above in §
5
.

1.4.
Related work.

This work is closely related to several recent preprints with different authors, although the results are mostly orthogonal. We have tried our best to represent them herein, and all misconceptions and mistakes in representing the work lie with the current author.

In
[
Ant25
, Definition 5.1]
a generalization of the derived algebraic contexts of Raksit are described. Unfortunately they still require compatibility of the
t
-structure with filtered colimits. However, it seems those definitions can be used to simplify the proofs presented in §
3.2
.

A less precise version of Proposition
4.51
appears as
[
LM25
, Remark B.5]
. The authors state that it follows from their Lemmas B
.2
and B
.3
, but the current author was unable to reconstruct their reasoning. In any case, our version of Proposition
4.51
is more precise and compatible with
[
Mou21
]
by construction.

The recent preprint
[
IY26
]
, studies results analogous to those presented in our §
3.3
. Their results are more general in the sense that they work with graded discrete rings which are complete for
homogeneous
ideals. Moreover they work with comodules over the group ring of torsion free
\mathbf{Z}
-modules, encompassing examples like
\mathbf{Z}[1/p]
which show up in their theory of graded perfectoid rings
[
IY25
]
. It should be possible to extend their results to animated rings using our techniques, but it is not clear to the author if there are any applications of such a theory. However, their paper was an inspiration to extend our classification to (formal) classifying stacks of diagonalizable group schemes.

After a preliminary draft of this work had been prepared, the author learned of the closely related but somewhat orthogonal work
[
GH25
]
. In that paper, the authors study a notion of ideals and completions in their ‘derived geometric contexts’ of
[
BBH25
]
, which (to the author’s understanding) is a geometric generalization of the ‘derived algebraic contexts’ of Raksit. The paper
[
GH25
]
, in particular works with a notion of
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
-stacks, where
\mathcal{C}
is a derived algebraic context and
\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
is the connective part. They then study a notion of ideals and completeness in this setting and give several deep applications to their notion of
derived geometries
. This is in contrast to our work, where we explicitly aim to set up a working theory of non-connective formal geometry with applications to prismatic cohomology. The main difference between their and our results is that they study quasi-coherent sheaves in this generality, which when specialized to the context of
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
studies completeness of modules over
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
, while we study complete objects in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
itself. We refer the reader to §
1
of their paper for a more in depth explanation of their results and technique than the author is capable of giving.

1.5.
Organization of the note.

The note is organized as follows. Fix an animated ring
A
and an ideal
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
. In §
2
we study a notion of
J
-complete derived algebras over
A
. The main observation is that
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is a localizaing subcategory of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
and that when one defines
\operatorname{Spf}(A)
as a prestack on all animated rings, with the formal direction being
J
-adic, then
\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))=\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.
In §
3
we study
J
-complete graded derived algebras over the animated ring
A
and prove various results which show that the
\infty\text{-category}

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
and
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
are comonadic over
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
and
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
in various settings.
In §
4
we study the notion of
\mathrm{DAlg}
on geometric (formal) stacks, by which we loosely mean those admitting a representable faithfully flat affine morphism from representable (formal) stacks. It is in this section where we apply the theory of the previous sections to prove the classification theorems.

1.6.
Notation and conventions.

We will freely use the language of
\infty
-categories and higher algebra as developed in
[
Lur09
]
and
[
Lur17
]
respectively. Our notation and conventions will thus reflect this.

1.6.1.

Geometric functors like
f_{*}
and
\Gamma(X,-)
will always be derived. Similarly, the symmetric monoidal functor on any symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category will simply be denoted
\otimes
.

1.6.2.

We denote by
\mathcal{S}
the
\infty
-category of spaces, or equivalently
\infty
-groupoids, or equivalently animae. We denote by
\mathrm{Sp}
the
\infty
-category of spectra, and by
\mathbf{S}
, the sphere spectrum .

1.6.3.

For the descent results we will need
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
, the
\infty
-category of all
\infty
-categories. We will also need
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}
the
\infty
-catgory of all presentable
\infty
-categories with colimit preserving functors between them, and its opposite category
\Pr^{R}
which has the same objects but limit preserving accessible functors.
\Pr^{L}
is a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category equipped with a Lurie tensor product
\mathcal{C}\otimes{\mathcal{D}}
for any
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{D}}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{L}.
Thus we will also contemplate
\mathrm{CAlg}(\Pr^{L})
, which is equivalently the
\infty\text{-category}
of of presentably symmetric monoidal
\infty\text{-categories}
.

1.6.4.

\mathrm{ARing}
denotes the
\infty
-category of animated rings and if
A
is an animated ring then we denote
\mathrm{ARing}_{A}
the under category
\mathrm{ARing}_{A/}
.

1.6.5.

We denote by
\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
denotes the
\infty
-category of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-algebras in
\mathrm{Sp}
. There is a functor
\Theta\colon\mathrm{ARing}\to\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
which sends an animated ring
A
to its underlying
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A^{\circ}.

1.6.6.

The
\infty
-category of modules
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is defined as the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{\circ}.}

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is symmetric monoidal and we write the tensor product as
\otimes_{A}
; in particular even when
A
is discrete, the tensor product is always derived.
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
carries a natural
t
-structure compatible with
\otimes_{A}
such that an
A
-module is (co)connective if and only if its image along the forgetful functor
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\mathrm{Sp}
is (co)connective. We denote by
(\mathrm{Mod}_{A})_{\geq 0}
(resp.
(\mathrm{Mod}_{A})_{\leq 0}
) the connective (resp. coconnective) parts of
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
.

1.6.7.

Let
\mathcal{C}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
be
\infty
-categories with
t
-structures. A functor
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to{\mathcal{D}}
is
left

t
-exact (resp.
right

t
-exact) if it preserves coconnective (resp. connective) objects. It is
t
-exact if it is both left and right
t
-exact.

1.6.8.

Let
A
be an animated ring and
f\in\pi_{0}A
. Then we write
A/\!\!/f
for the tensor product
\mathbf{Z}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}[x]}A
, where the latter is the algebra obtained by freely setting
f=0
in
A

6
6

6

Note that this is not independent of the choice of generators. For example
A/\!\!/0=A\oplus A[1]\neq A
unless
A
itself is the zero ring.
. This is also equivalent as a module to
\mathrm{cofib}(A\xrightarrow{f}A).
For a sequence of elements
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})
we write
A/\!\!/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})
for the tensor product
A\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]}\mathbf{Z}
. As a module this is equivalent to
\bigotimes_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{cofib}(A\xrightarrow{f_{i}}A).
For an
A
-module
M
, we denote by
M/\!\!/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})
the tensor product
M\otimes_{A}A/\!\!/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}).

1.6.9.

On occasion it would be necessary to take quotients of discrete rings
A
by sequences
f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}\in A
. In this case we write
A/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})
for the quotient. Similarly if
M
is a discrete
A
-module and
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})
is a sequence of elements of
A
then the classical quotient is
M/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}).
Note that even if
A
is discrete,
A/\!\!/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})\neq A/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})
and the latter is the Koszul complex on the sequence
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}).

1.6.10.

Let
A
be an animated ring and let
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
be a finitely generated ideal. We say a module
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is
J
-complete if after choosing generators
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})=J
, the canonical map
M\to\lim_{n}M/\!\!/(f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{r}^{n})
is an isomorphism. This is independent of the choice of generators. We let
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
the full subcategory
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
of
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
spanned by
J
-complete modules. This is a localizing subcategory of
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
and the left adjoint to the inclusion
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is given by completion
(-)^{\wedge}_{J}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

1.6.11.

Suppose
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
. Then recall from
[
Lur17
, Definition 7.2.2.10]
that is called flat if
\pi_{0}M
is a flat
\pi_{0}(A)
module and the natural action map
\pi_{*}(A)\otimes_{\pi_{0}(A)}\pi_{0}(M)\to\pi_{*}(M)
is an isomorphism of
\mathbf{Z}
-graded abelian groups (in particular,
M
is connective). It is faithfully flat if it is flat and
\pi_{0}M
is a faithfully flat
\pi_{0}A
-module.

1.6.12.

For
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
finitely generated, we say that a module
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is
J
-completely (faithfully) flat if after choosing generators
J=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})
the module
M/\!\!/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})
is a (faithfully) flat module over
A/\!\!/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})
. This is equivalent to
M\otimes_{A}(-)
being
t
-exact on
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{tors}}
, the
\infty\text{-category}
of
J
-torsion
A
modules, for the induced
t
-structure from
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
and thus is independent of choice of generators.

1.6.13.

Faithfully flat maps over an animated ring
A
form a Grothendieck topology on
(\mathrm{ARing}_{A})^{\mathrm{op}}
which we call the fpqc topology and denote
(\mathrm{ARing}_{A})_{\mathrm{fpqc}}.
We will use that the assignment
A\mapsto\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is a (hyper)sheaf on
(\mathrm{ARing}_{A})_{\mathrm{fpqc}}.

1.6.14.

A prestack over an animated ring
A
is simply an accessible functor
X\colon\mathrm{ARing}_{A}\to\mathcal{S}
. A stack is an accessible hypersheaf
X\colon(\mathrm{ARing}_{A})_{\mathrm{fpqc}}\to\mathcal{S}.
While we will work in the generality of prestacks, the objects of interest will be genuine stacks.

1.6.15.

The category of quasi-coherent sheaves on a prestack
X\colon\mathrm{ARing}_{A}\to\mathcal{S}
is defined by right Kan extension by the formula

\mathrm{QCoh}(X)=\lim_{{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)\to X}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}

where the limit is over all animated rings with a map to
X.
The
\infty\text{-category}

\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
carries a
t
-strucutre wherein a module is connective if and only if it is after pullback to
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
for all
R
appearing in the limit diagram. The co-connective part is defined by orthogonality and does not yield an easy description except in specific cases.

1.6.16.

We state our conventions on set theory. In the main body of the paper the only point at which we need to contend with set theory is when computing limits for right Kan extensions as in the formula in
1.6.15
. However this will not be an issue as in our cases of interest, the limits will always be computed on a small category indexed by the non-negative integers. On the other hand, in Appendix
A
, we need to work with arbitrary prestacks and in that case we will work with prestacks valued in the
\infty
-category
\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
of large spaces, defined as in
[
Lur09
, Remark 1.2.16.4]
.

KL
]
, which is then used in our Appendix
A
, also mostly explained to us by Kubrak, as well as gave some useful comments on an earlier draft.

\infty\text{-categories}
over the years, which gave the author the confidence to pursue the topics herein.

2401536

and from S.E. Warschawski Professorship under Kiran Kedlaya.

2.
Derived algebras on formal spectra.

Notation 2.1
.

For the rest of this section, fix an animated ring
A
and
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
a finitely generated ideal.

Goal 2.2
.

We are interested in defining an
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
of
J
-complete derived
A
-algebras with the following properties

(1)

\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is the full subcategory of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
consisting of derived algebras whose underlying modules are
J
-complete.

(2)

\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is presentably symmetric monoidal and the inclusion functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
is a localization and the right adjoint
(-)^{\wedge}_{J}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is given by completion at the level of modules in the sense of
1.6.10
.

(3)

The forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is monadic with left adjoint
(-)^{\wedge}_{J}\circ\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A}
.

(4)

The forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
preserves all colimits.

(5)

When we consider
\operatorname{Spf}(A)
, defined as the subfunctor of
{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
, sending
B\in\mathrm{ARing}
to the subspace of
\mathrm{Maps}(A,B)
annihilating some power of
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
, then
\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))
, defined via the general procedure of right Kan extensions, agrees with
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
.

(6)

On the category of animated
A
-algebras, the functor
B\mapsto\mathrm{DAlg}_{B}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
satisfies
J
-completely flat descent in the sense of
1.6.12
.

In the next few subsections we will show that the naive definition of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
as those derived algebras which satisfy property
(1)
above accomplishes all the goals highlighted in Goal
2.2
. We prove the first
5
in this section and the last one is established in the next one.

2.1.
J
-complete derived algebras.

Definition 2.3
.

We define
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
as the full subcategory of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
consisting of derived algebras where the underlying functor
U\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
admits a factorisation
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\xrightarrow{U^{J}}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
where the last functor is the canonical inclusion of
J
-complete
A
-modules.

We begin by giving another definition of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
which makes the functorialities of the above constructions apparent.

Lemma 2.4
.

There is a canonical pullback diagram in
\Pr^{R}
the
\infty
-category of presentable
\infty
-categories along with right adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}}

where the bottom horizontal arrow is the inclusion
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
and the right vertical arrow is
U\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}.

Proof.

Let us write
\mathcal{C}
for the
\infty\text{-category}
of Definition
2.3
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
for the pullback in the lemma. Note that the functor
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
is full faithful being pulled back along a fully faithful functor
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
.
Therefore the canonical functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
induced by the universal property of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is fully faithful as the composite
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
is fully faithful. It remains to check that the functor is essentially surjective. To show this it remains to check that a pair
(R,M,\sigma)
consisting of
R\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{A},M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
and an isomorphism
\sigma\colon M\to R
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
comes from
\mathcal{C}.
But
\mathcal{C}
consists of derived algebras whose underlying modules are
J
-complete, thus upto isomorphism we may pick
(R,U(R),\mathrm{id}_{R})
where
U\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is the forgetful functor.
∎

Proposition 2.5
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
satisfies the following properties

(1)

\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is a presentable
\infty\text{-category}
and therefore admits all limits and colimits.

(2)

The inclusion
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
is a fully faithful right adjoint, and therefore admits a left adjoint
L\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.
In other words it is a localizing subcategory of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

(3)

\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
inherits a symmetric monoidal structure from
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
given by
L(R\otimes_{A}S)
and so that
L\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is symmetric monoidal.

Proof.

(1)

It follows from Lemma
2.4
that
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is presentable as the pullback diagram is taken in
\Pr^{R}
and can be computed after forgetting to
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}.
Since a presentable
\infty\text{-category}
has all limits and colimits, the second conclusion follows.

(2)

The first claim can be checked in two ways, either from Definition
2.3
or from the pullback diagram in Lemma
2.4
. In the latter case note that pullbacks of fully faithful functors remain fully faithful. The inlcusion is a right adjoint since the pullback diagram is taken in
\Pr^{R}
and therefore admits a left adjoint
L\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

(3)

Recall from
[
Lur09
, Remark 5.2.7.5]
that colimits in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
can be computed in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
and then completed. This allows us to induce a cocartesian monoidal structure of
[
Lur17
, Definition 2.4.0.1]
on
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
, which by the above is given by
L(R\otimes_{A}S).
The part about symmetric monoidality follows from the fact that for any derived
A
-algebra
R
the functor
R\to L(R)
is an
J
-complete equivalence at the level of module. In particular the fiber (as modules) is
J
-local. But as explained in
[
Lur11c
, Remark 4.2.6]
, if
M
is an
J
-local
A
-module then
N\otimes_{A}M
is
J
-local. it follows that the canonical morphism of derived algebras
L(R\otimes_{A}S)\to L(L(R)\otimes_{A}L(S))
is an equivalence.

∎

Our goal now is to prove that the right adjoint
L\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
can be computed at the level of modules.

Construction 2.6
.

[A formula for completion.]
Fix generators
J=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}).

For each
n>0
consider the derived
A
-algebra

A_{n}:=A/\!\!/(f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{r}^{n})

of
1.6.8
.

Now let
R
be a derived
A
-algebra. We will define a
J
-complete derived
A
-algebra
R^{\wedge}_{J}
as follows.

Since tensor products of derived
A
-algebras remain derived
A
-algebras we set

R_{n}=R\otimes_{A}A_{n}.

Then set

R^{\wedge}_{J}=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}R_{n}.

(2.1)

Since
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
has all limits, and they are computed at the level of modules, we see that
R^{\wedge}_{J}
is a derived algebra. Further from Definition
2.3
we see that
R^{\wedge}_{J}
is clearly
J
-complete, for example from
1.6.10
.

There is a canonical morphism of derived
A
-algebras

R\to R^{\wedge}_{J}

(2.2)

We next show that the procedure in Construction
2.6
provides an explicit formula for the completion.

Lemma 2.7
.

The canonical morphism
R\to R^{\wedge}_{J}
of Equation
2.2
exhibits the target as a left adjoint to the inclusion
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}.

Proof.

Note that each ring
A_{n}
in Construction
2.6
is
J
-nilpotent and therefore
J
-complete. A similar statement holds for
R\otimes_{A}A_{n}.
Thus the algebras
R\otimes_{A}A_{n}
are all
J
-complete. Now limits of
J
-complete derived algebras remain
J
-complete as explained in the proof of Proposition
2.5
. Thus we see that
R^{\wedge}_{J}
is a
J
-complete derived algebra.

Now as explained in the proof of
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.2.7.4]
we may check that for any
J
-complete derived algebra
R
, the canonical map
R\to R^{\wedge}_{J}
is an isomorphism. For this we may forget to
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
in which case we are reduced to checking the following fact: Let
M
be in
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
, then the canonical morphism
M\to\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}M/\!\!/(f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{r}^{n})
is an isomorphism. But this is well known and may be deduced, for example, from
[
BS15
, Lemma 3.4.12]
, after specializing to the punctual
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{S}
of spaces and noting that the argument in
loc.cit.
works formally for animated rings.
∎

Notation 2.8
.

In light of Lemma
2.7
, we will henceforth denote the left adjoint
L\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
by
(-)^{\wedge}_{J}

with the understanding
that the universal property of left adjoints shows that it is independent of the set of generators.

We thank Ben Antieau for suggesting the statement of the next corollary.

Corollary 2.9
.

The diagram in Lemma
2.4
is left adjointable. More precisely, the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{J}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{J}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{J}}

canonically commutes.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Lemma
2.7
by examining the explicit formula for completion.
∎

Corollary 2.10
.

The functor
U^{J}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is symmetric monoidal.

Proof.

This follows again from Corollary
2.9
, along with point
(3)
in Proposition
2.10
and the fact that
U\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is symmetric monoidal.
∎

Notation 2.11
.

In light of Corollary
2.10
we will now denote the tensor product of two derived
A
-algebras
R
and
S
by
R\widehat{\otimes}_{A}S
as no confusion can arise.

Construction 2.12
.

Note that the
\infty\text{-category}
of
J
-complete
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-algebras over
A
also sits in the pullback diagram in
\Pr^{R}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}.}

Note that this gives
\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
a cocartesian monoidal structure admitted by
J
-completing the usual tensor product in
\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}.

Using from point
(c)
of
[
Rak26
, Notation 4.2.28]
, that there is a forgetful ‘underlying’
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-algebra functor
\Theta\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}
which is symmetric monoidal, conservative and preserves all limits and colimits.

Thus, by the pullback description of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
in Lemma
2.4
, there is an evident forgetful functor
\Theta^{J}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
fitting into a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta^{J}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta}

where the horizontal arrows the canonical inclusions.

Proposition 2.13
.

The functor
\theta^{J}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is conservative, preserves all limits and colimits and is symmetric monoidal.

Proof.

It is clear that the functor
\theta^{J}
is conservative. The functor
\Theta^{J}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is right adjoint as it is induced entirely in
\Pr^{R}
and hence preserves all limits. It remains to show the case of colimits. There is an evident commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta^{J}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{J}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{J}}

obtained from the right adjointability of the second diagram in Construction
2.12
. This right adjointability can be seen from Corollary
2.9
and because completions of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-algebras can be computed at the level of modules by
[
Lur11c
, Remark 4.2.6]
.

For the purpose of this proof let us denote the inclusion
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
by
i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}
and the inclusion
\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}
by
i_{\mathrm{CAlg}}.

Now let
F\colon K\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
be a colimit diagram.
Then we have that the colimit in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is computed by
(\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{K}(i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}(F))^{\wedge}_{J}
.
On the other hand note that

\Theta(\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{K}(i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}(F)))=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{K}(\Theta\circ i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}(F))=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{K}(i_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\Theta^{J}(F))

where the first equality comes because, as recalled in Construction
2.12
,
\Theta
commutes with all colimits, and the second equality follows because of the commutativity of the second diagram in Construction
2.12
.

Completing the rightmost expression we obtain
\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{K}(i_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\Theta^{J}(F))^{\wedge}
which is the colimit of the diagram
\Theta^{J}\circ F\colon K\to\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
. But this agrees with
\Theta^{J}((\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{K}(i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}(F))^{\wedge}_{J})
by commutativity of the displayed diagram in this proof.

Lasty, the symmetric monoidality of
\Theta^{J}
follows by the fact that it preserves colimits and both the source and target are equipped with their respective cocartesian monoidal structure.
∎

Remark 2.14
.

Let
R\leftarrow S\rightarrow T
be a span in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
. Then Proposition
2.13
implies that the pushout can be computed as
R\widehat{\otimes}_{S}T
and agrees with the completed tensor product of the underlying
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-algebras.

We can now establish functoriality of the assignment on animated
A
-algebras which sends
B\mapsto\mathrm{DAlg}_{B}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

Remark 2.15
.

Let
B\to C
be a map of animated
A
-algebras. Then the functor which sends
\mathrm{DAlg}_{B}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{C}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is given by
R\mapsto R\widehat{\otimes}_{B}C.
Indeed it suffices to observe that the completed tensor product of a
J
-complete
A
-algebra
R
with another (possibly non-complete)
A
-algebra
T
is
J
-complete. But this follows because
J
-completion is symmetric monoidal as explained in point
(3)
of Proposition
2.5
.

We end by establishing the monadicity of the forgetful functor
U^{J}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

Proposition 2.16
.

The forgetful functor
U^{J}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is monadic and the adjoint is given by
(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A})^{\wedge}_{J}:=(-)^{\wedge}_{J}\circ\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{A}.

Proof.

We will use the coarse Barr-Beck-Lurie criteria of
[
Lur17
, Theorem 4.7.0.3]
. First note that as already established
U^{J}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is a right adjoint between presentable
\infty\text{-categories}
and so it admits a left adjoint. We will discuss the left adjoint soon. But first we verify the other criteria in the monadicity theorem. Note that
U^{J}
is conservative by construction.
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is presentable hence admits geometric realizations. It suffices to show that
U^{J}
preserves all geometric realizations. We will follow the same strategy as in the proof of
2.13
.
To this end denote by
i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}
the inclusion
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
and by
i_{\mathrm{Mod}}
the inclusion
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}.

Now let
X_{\bullet}\in\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
be a simplicial object and
|X_{\bullet}|
be its geometric realiszation. Then note first that

U^{J}|X_{\bullet}|=U^{J}((|i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}(X)^{\bullet}|)^{\wedge}_{J})=(U|i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}(X)^{\bullet}|)^{\wedge}_{J},

where the first equality is by the definition of colimits in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
and the second by the commutativity of the diagram in Corollary
2.9
wherein
U^{J}\circ(-)^{\wedge}_{J}\simeq(-)^{\wedge}_{J}\circ U.

Further note that
U\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
commutes with all sifted colimits by point
(c)
of
[
Rak26
, Notation 4.2.28]
. Thus

(U|i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}(X)^{\bullet}|)^{\wedge}_{J}=|U\circ i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}(X)^{\bullet}|^{\wedge}_{J}.

Now by commutativity of the defining diagram in Lemma
2.4
we have
U\circ i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}\simeq i_{\mathrm{Mod}}\circ U^{J}
whence we obtain that

|U\circ i_{\mathrm{DAlg}}(X)^{\bullet}|^{\wedge}_{J}=|i_{\mathrm{Mod}}\circ U^{J}(X)|^{\wedge}_{J}.

But the right hand side is precisely the definition of
|U^{J}(X^{\bullet})|
as desired.

It remains to compute the monad which means for us to compute the left adjoint. To this end note that if
R
is a
J
-complete derived
A
-algebra then we have the following equalities for any
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}

\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}(M,R)=\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}(M,R)=\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}}(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}(M),R)

where the first equality is because
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is fully faithful inside
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
, the second one is because
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
is a left adjoint. Now since completion is left adjoint we conclude that the righmost mapping space is equivalent to
\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}(M)^{\wedge}_{J},R)
and therefore we have a canonical equivalence of mapping spaces

\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}(M,R)\simeq\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}(M)^{\wedge}_{J},R).

∎

Remark 2.17
.

Note that the notion of a
derived algebraic context
due to Raksit
[
Rak26
, Defintion 4.2.1]
while very useful is somewhat strict of a notion. For example in the sequel we would like to rely on results presented in this section but for
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
and
\mathrm{FilMod}_{A}
respectively. It would be very desireable to clarify the theory as follows

(1)

A derived algebraic context
\mathcal{C}
is
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
-linear object in
\Pr^{L}
. It would be very useful to make the notion flexible enough to obtain functoriality for the base changes
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}.

(2)

Moreover it would be desireable to obtain the results in this section via base changes of the
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
-linear category
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
along
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
where the map is given by completion.

Both of the results above would imply that one can do the completed theory presented here rather formally even in the case of completed graded and filtered
A
-modules, a notion we will introduce piecemeal in the sequel.

2.2.
Derived algebras on
J
-adic formal spectra.

We continue with the notation established in Notation
2.1
.
Our goal is the following

Goal 2.18
.

Consider the prestack
\operatorname{Spf}(A)\colon\mathrm{ARing}_{\mathrm{fpqc}}\to\mathcal{S}
which sends any animated ring
B
to the summand of
\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{ARing}}(A,B)
consisting of maps which annihilate some power of
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
.
Our goal is to show that when we define
\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))
by right Kan extensions, then there is an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))=\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

We will achieve Goal
2.18
by first establishing the analgous statement for (the right Kan extension of)
\mathrm{QCoh}(-)
. The result will then follow from conservativity of the forgetful functor.

Remark 2.19
.

In the forthcoming Construction
2.20
, we define
\mathrm{DAlg}(X)
on a prestack
X
simply by Kan extensions.
However, one may consider the coCartesian fibration
\mathrm{Mod}\to\mathrm{ARing}
and note that
[
Rak26
, Remark 4.2.29]
also implies that one has an endo-transformation
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}
of the coCartesian fibration
\mathrm{Mod}\to\mathrm{ARing}
i.e. it preserves all coCartesian arrows. This gives a natural endofunctor
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{X}\in\mathrm{End}(\mathrm{QCoh}(X))
for any prestack
X
, which one can check by hand to be monadic (or by using more precise results of Kubrak-Li presented in Construction
A.3
). Then one may contemplate the difference between
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{X}}(\mathrm{QCoh}(X))
and
\mathrm{DAlg}(X).
Indeed the definition via the global monad
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{X}
is useful in calculations, as done in
[
Pet25
, § 2]
, while that of Construction
2.20
is useful in proving results like our Corollary
2.31
.
In fact we will show that both these constructions are equivalent in our Appendix
A
, using ideas of D. Kubrak.

Construction 2.20

(Derived algebras on prestacks)
.

For any animated ring
R\in\mathrm{ARing}
, the assignment
R\mapsto\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
forms a
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
-valued functor by
[
Rak26
, Remark 4.2.29]
. It follows by applying the
\infty\text{-category}
Grothendieck construction in the form of Lurie’s unstraightening
[
Lur09
, Theorem 3.2.0.1]
that one gets a coCartesian fibration
\mathrm{DAlg}\to\mathrm{ARing}
. Using the dual of
[
Lur11b
, Proposition 2.7.6.]
in the form of Remark
2.7.8
of
loc.cit.
, we may
define

\mathrm{DAlg}(X)
for any prestack
X
on
\mathrm{ARing}
via the formula

\mathrm{DAlg}(X)=\lim_{{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)\to X}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}.

This definition has the following features.

(1)

For any stack
X
, there is a forgetful functor
U_{X}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
which is monadic by Lemma
A.1
.

(2)

The functor
U_{X}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
factors over
\mathrm{CAlg}(X):=\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{QCoh}(X))
is conservative and preserves all limits and colimits.

(3)

Let
L_{X}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}(X)\to\mathrm{DAlg}(X)
be the left adjoint. Then the monad
U_{X}\circ L_{X}
on
\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
is given by the global monad
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{X}
explained in Remark
2.19
(or more precisely in Construction
A.3
) by Proposition
A.4
.

We now need an analogue of
[
Lur18
, Lemma 8.1.2.2]
for animated rings complete along a finitely generated ideal. In the setting of
derived
algebraic geometry this lemma can be substantially simplified.

Lemma 2.21

()
.

Let
A
be an animated ring and
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
a finitely generated ideal. Then there exists an
\mathbf{Z}_{>0}
-indexed tower
\ldots\to A_{4}\to A_{3}\to A_{2}\to A_{1}
in the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{ARing}_{A}
with the following properties

(1)

The maps
A_{i+1}\to A_{i}
induce a surjection on
\pi_{0}(A_{i+1})\to\pi_{0}(A_{i})
with nilpotent kernel.

(2)

For every animated ring
B
, the canonical map

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{ARing}}(A_{n},B)\to\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{ARing}}(A,B)

induces a homotopy equivalence of the source with the summand consisting of those maps
A\to B
which annihilate some power of
J
i.e. with
\operatorname{Spf}(A)
.

(3)

Each of the animated rings
A_{n}
are perfect when regarded as an
A
-module.

Proof.

As in the proof of
[
Lur18
, Lemma 8.1.2.2]
it suffices to construct for each
x\in\pi_{0}(A)
a tower of rings
\ldots\to A_{4}(x)\to A_{3}(x)\to A_{2}(x)\to A_{1}(x)
satisfying the properties in the lemma i.e. prove the lemma for
J=(x).
Then we can conclude the case of arbitrary finitely generated ideals by choosing generators
J=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})
and setting
A_{n}=A_{n}(x_{1})\otimes_{A}A_{n}(x_{2})\otimes_{A}\ldots\otimes_{A}A_{n}(x_{r}).

In fact, since we are in the animated setting, our proof is far simpler since quotients exist as described for example in
1.6.8
and for
x\in\pi_{0}(A)
we may simply take
A_{n}(x)=A/\!\!/x^{n}.
It remains to check that the the listed properties in the statement of the lemma are satisfied. The properties
(1)
and
(3)
are clearly true. Thus it remains to verify property
(2).
The argument here matches the argument in
loc.cit.

First fix an
n>0
and consider the canonical map
A\to A/\!\!/x^{n}.
Suppose
B
is an animated ring and
\varphi\colon A\to B
a morphism. Then the space of factorizations of
\varphi\colon A\to B
through
A\to A/\!\!/x^{n}
is identified with the space of paths in
\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathbf{Z}}(\mathbf{Z}[w],B)\simeq B
between
\varphi(x^{n})
and
0.
Indeed this follows immediately from the universal property of
A/\!\!/x^{n}
as described in
1.6.8
. It follows that the homotopy fiber
P^{n}
of
\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{ARing}}(A_{n},B)\to\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{ARing}}(A,B)
over
\varphi
is identified with
\mathrm{Maps}_{B}(\varphi(x^{n}),0)
inside the
\infty
-category of the underlying space of
B.
Note that then the homotopy fiber of

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{ARing}}(A_{n},B)\to\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{ARing}}(A,B)

is identified with
P_{\infty}:=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}P_{n}
where the transition map is given by multiplication by
\varphi(x).

If
\varphi(x)
is not nilpotent in
B
then
P_{n}
is empty for each
n
and so there’s nothing to prove. So assume that
P_{n}
is non-empty for some
n
. Then we observe that
\pi_{m}(P_{n})=\pi_{m+1}(B).
Indeed this follows from noting that
\mathrm{Maps}_{B}(\varphi(x^{n}),0)
is a torsor over
\mathrm{Maps}_{B}(0,0)=\Omega_{0}B
where the right hand side is the space of loops based at
0
.
So now we have

\pi_{m}(P_{\infty})=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}\pi_{m}(P_{n})=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}(\pi_{m+1}(B)\xrightarrow{\varphi(x)}\pi_{m+1}(B)\xrightarrow{\varphi(x)}\pi_{m+1}(B)\to\ldots)

but the right hand side is
0
since
\varphi(x)
is nilpotent.
∎

Remark 2.22
.

In light of
the proof of Lemma
2.21
, whenever we choose a tower
\ldots\to A_{3}\to A_{2}\to A_{1}
for the animated ring
A
, we have
implicitly chosen a set of generators for
J
.

Corollary 2.23
.

Let
A
an animated ring and
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
be finitely generated then
\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}A)=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{n}}
and
\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}A)=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A_{n}}
where the
A_{n}
are as constructed in Lemma
2.21
.

Proof.

Keeping in mind Remark
2.22
, both of these follow from noting that the category defined by the tower
{\mathrm{Spec}}(A_{1})\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(A_{2})\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(A_{3})\to\ldots
are final in the indexing
\infty\text{-category}

\mathrm{DAff}_{X}
by Lemma
2.21
and so the limit of Construction
2.20
and
1.6.15
can be computed on this category.
∎

Construction 2.24
.

Note that by the explicit description of
\operatorname{Spf}(A)
as a prestack on
\mathrm{ARing}_{\mathrm{fpqc}}
there is a canonical morphism of prestacks

\eta_{A}\colon\operatorname{Spf}(A)\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(A).

This induces a pullback morphism

(\eta_{A}^{*})_{\mathrm{Mod}}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}({\mathrm{Spec}}(A))\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))

and similarly

(\eta_{A}^{*})_{\mathrm{DAlg}}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}({\mathrm{Spec}}(A))\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A)).

By construction, it follows that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}({\mathrm{Spec}}(A))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\eta_{A}^{*})_{\mathrm{DAlg}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{\operatorname{Spf}(A)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\eta_{A}^{*})_{\mathrm{Mod}}}

Using Construction
2.24
we now prove an analogue of
[
Lur18
, Theorem 8.3.4.4]
in the setting of formal derived algebraic geometry.

Remark 2.25
.

As stated in
[
Lur18
, Remark 8.3.4.5]
, in this setting we do not need a connectivity hypothesis while working in derived algebraic geometry. Compare also
[
BS22
, Footnote 12]
. Note that this result appears in a restricted setting in
[
Sta25
,
Tag 0H0L
]
7
7

7

Note that the Noetherian hypothesis in the referenced tag is inessential and can be replaced by a notion of ideals generated by weakly proregular sequences. See
[
Sta25
,
Tag 0H1E
]
, with the point being that for a weakly proregular sequence in any discrete ring
A
, the pro-systems
\{A/\!\!/(x_{1}^{n},\ldots,x_{r}^{n})\}
and
\{A/(x^{n}_{1},\ldots,x^{n}_{r})\}
are pro-isomorphic.
and
[
AKN23
, Appendix A.]
in the classical setting of bounded
p
-torsion rings, and should certainly be well known over animated rings. However, we do not know a reference in this generality, although note Remark
2.32
.

Theorem 2.26
.

The pullback
(\eta_{A}^{*})_{\mathrm{Mod}}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}({\mathrm{Spec}}(A))\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))
restricts to an equivalence on
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}

Proof.

Choose a set of generators
J=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})
and set
A_{n}=A/\!\!/(x_{1}^{n},\ldots,x_{r}^{n}).

By Corollary
2.23
we can identify
\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))\simeq\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{n}}
and the functor
(\eta_{A}^{*})_{\mathrm{Mod}}
may be identified with the functor
F\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{n}}
sending
M\mapsto\{M\otimes_{A}A_{n}\}
. This functor admits a right adjoint
G\colon\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{n}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
sending
\{M_{n}\}\mapsto\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}M_{n}.
Since each of the
M_{n}
are modules over the
J
-nilpotent animated rings
A_{n}
it follows that
\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}M_{n}
is a
J
-complete module
A
and lands in
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

By abuse of notation we denote the restriction of
F
to
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
also by
F
and so we have an adjunction

F\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\rightleftarrows\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{n}}\colon G.

It follows that the unit
\eta_{M}\colon M\to G\circ F(M)=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}M\otimes_{A}A_{n}
is an isomorphism for all
J
-complete modules
M
. Thus we have a fully faithful embedding
F\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{n}}.
For formal reasons,
8
8

8

Let
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to{\mathcal{D}}
be a functor of
\infty\text{-categories}
with right adjoint
G\colon{\mathcal{D}}\to\mathcal{C}
. If
F
is fully faithful, and
G
is conservative, then
F
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories. Indeed full faithfulness of
F
gives that
\eta\colon\mathrm{id}_{C}\to G\circ F
is an equivalence. Now it suffices to show that the counit
\varepsilon\colon F\circ G\to\mathrm{id}_{D}
is an equivalence. By the triangle identity we have that
G(\varepsilon)\circ\eta_{G}=\mathrm{id}_{G}
and so since
\eta_{G}
is an equivalence, we learn that
G(\varepsilon)
is an equivalence. By conservativity of
G
we conclude that
\varepsilon
is an equivalence. A similar proof holds when
G
is fully faithful and
F
is conservative.
it suffices to show that
G
is conservative.

Since we are in a stable
\infty\text{-category}
it suffices to show that if
G(\{M_{n}\})=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}M_{n}=0
then
M_{n}=0
for all
n>0.
This is a consequence of Lemma
2.29
.
∎

In Lemma
2.29
, we will prove an animated analogue of Lemma
[
BS15
, 3.5.4]
, proved there for discrete Noetherian rings in a classical topos.
Before we do that we need a calculation of certain pro-systems defined by Koszul complexes.

Lemma 2.27
.

In the situation of Theorem
2.26
, consider the pro-system given by
\{A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}\}_{n>0}
and the constant pro-system
\{A_{1}\}_{n>0}
. Then
\{A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}\}_{n>0}
is pro-isomorphic to
\{A_{1}\}_{n>0}
.

Proof.

Note that for any
n>0
we have
A_{n}=A/\!\!/f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f^{n}_{r}
is obtained by freely setting the elements
f^{n}_{i}=0
for
1\leq i\leq r
.

In particular if
M
is any
A
-module, then the action of
x_{i}^{n}
on
M\otimes_{A}A_{n}
factors through
0.

Since tensors are computed at the level of modules, we may also write the underlying as
\bigotimes_{i=1}^{i=r}\mathrm{cofib}(A\xrightarrow{x_{i}^{n}}A).

Thus we compute

\displaystyle A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}

\displaystyle=(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{cofib}(A\xrightarrow{x_{i}^{n}}A))\otimes_{A}(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{r}A/\!\!/x_{i})

\displaystyle=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{r}\mathrm{cofib}(A/\!\!/x_{i}\xrightarrow{x_{i}^{n}}A/\!\!/x_{i})

\displaystyle=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{r}(A/\!\!/x_{i}\oplus A/\!\!/x_{i}[1])

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{r}A_{1}^{\oplus\binom{r}{k}}[k].

(2.3)

Thus we learn that the terms in the tower
\{A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}\}
are independent of
n.

Further from
2.2
there is an obvious morphism of pro-systems
\{A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}\}_{n>0}\to\{A_{1}\}_{n>0}
corresponding to projection on the first factor and a section
\{A_{1}\}_{n>0}\to\{A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}\}_{n>0}.

It remains to check that this induced morphism is actually a pro-isomorphism. For this it suffices to show that for any
n>1
the morphism
A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}\to A_{n-1}\otimes_{A}A_{1}
is identity on the first factor of
2.2
and
0
on the other factors. This will prove the result as it is now clear that the morphism of pro-systems
\{A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}\}_{n>0}\to\{A_{1}\}_{n>0}
is an isomorphism.

So now we prove the fact about the morphism being
\mathrm{id}
on the first factor and
0
on positive factors.

Note that the morphism
A_{n}\to A_{n-1}
is obtained by base change along
\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]\xrightarrow{x_{i}\to f_{i}}A
of the universal morphism

\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]/(x_{1}^{n},\ldots,x_{r}^{n})\to\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]/(x_{1}^{n-1},\ldots,x_{r}^{n-1})

(2.4)

The tensor product
A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}\to A_{n-1}\otimes_{A}A_{1}
is obtained by a further base change along
A\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]}\mathbf{Z}
. Thus to understand the morphism we may just base change
2.4
along
-\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]}\mathbf{Z}
.

Writing

\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]/(x_{1}^{n},\ldots,x_{r}^{n})=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{i=r}\mathrm{cofib}(\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]\xrightarrow{x_{i}^{n}}\mathbf{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}])

and viewing the induced morphism of
2.4
by coming, after iterated tensoring, from the complex

\hbox to156.52pt{\vbox to54.66pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 78.26149pt\lower-26.31947pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

we see that the derivation of
2.2
, gives that the induces morphism

\bigoplus_{k=0}^{r}\mathbf{Z}^{\oplus\binom{r}{k}}[k]\to\bigoplus_{k=0}^{r}\mathbf{Z}^{\oplus\binom{r}{k}}[k]

is
\mathrm{id}
on the first factor and zero on all factors with a postive degree. After base change to
A_{1}
we conclude the same for the induced morphism

\bigoplus_{k=0}^{r}A_{1}^{\oplus\binom{r}{k}}[k]\to\bigoplus_{k=0}^{r}A_{1}^{\oplus\binom{r}{k}}[k].
