Corollary 2.28
.

Let
k>0
, then the pro-systems
\{A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{k}\}_{n>0}\to\{A_{k}\}_{n>0}
are pro-isomorphic.

Proof.

We may look at the sub-pro-system of
\{A_{n}\}_{n>0}
consisting of the tower
\{A_{mk}\}_{m>0}
. Then by Lemma
2.27
, the pro-systems
\{A_{mk}\otimes_{A}A_{k}\}_{m>0}\to\{A_{k}\}_{m>0}
are pro-isomorphic.
Note that
\{A_{mk}\otimes_{A}A_{k}\}
is final in the co-filtered system
\{A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{k}\}_{n>0}
, thus we can conclude the result.
∎

Lemma 2.29
.

In the setting of Theorem
2.26
, any tower
\{M_{n}\}\in\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{n}}
we have
G(\{M_{n}\})\otimes_{A}A_{k}=(\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}M_{n})\otimes_{A}A_{k}=M_{k}.

Proof.

Note that each
A_{k}
is a perfect (hence dualizable)
A
-module by point
(3)
of Lemma
2.21
.
Thus we have that
(\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}M_{n})\otimes_{A}A_{k}=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}(M_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{k}).
Therefore it suffices to verify that the pro-system
\{M_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{k}\}
is pro-isomorphic to the constant system
\{M_{k}\}.

Now note that we have a change of rings pro-isomorphism
\{M_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{k}\}\simeq\{M_{n}\otimes_{A_{n}}A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{k}\}.

We first assume that
k=1.
In this case we observe that the explicit proof of lemma
2.27
, in particular
2.2
, shows that for each
n>0
the algebra
A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}
is equivalent to
A_{1}\oplus C
where
C
is
\binom{r}{k}-1
shifted copies of
A_{1}
and the number of shifts. Thus tensoring
M_{n}
with
A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}
gives
M_{n}\otimes_{A_{n}}A_{1}\oplus C^{\prime}
where
C^{\prime}
is
\binom{r}{k}-1
shifted copies of
M_{n}\otimes_{A_{n}}A_{1}
.
Note that, after this identification, the induced map in the tower
M_{n}\otimes_{A_{n}}A_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{1}\to M_{n-1}\otimes_{A_{n-1}}A_{n-1}\otimes_{A}A_{1}
is the one coming from

M_{n}\otimes_{A_{n}}A_{1}\to M_{n-1}\otimes_{A_{n-1}}A_{1}

on the first factor and zero on the rest of the factors.

We may apply similar reasoning for the tower
\{M_{n}\otimes_{A_{n}}A_{1}\}_{n>0}.

Thus the natural projection map again is a pro-isomorphism.

Now suppose
k>1
. In this case we again look at the final sub-pro-system given by the indices
\{M_{mk}\otimes_{A_{mk}}A_{k}\}_{m>0}
and similarly for
\{M_{mk}\otimes_{A_{mk}}A_{k}\}_{m>0}
and repeat the reasoning.

Therefore
\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}(M_{n}\otimes_{A}A_{k})=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}(M_{n}\otimes_{A_{n}}A_{k})
and the second limit is eventually constant with value
M_{k}.
So we conclude.
∎

Remark 2.30
.

In
[
Sta25
,
Tag 0GZI
]
a different proof of Lemma
2.29
is given. We are not sure if it applies in our situation as the models of pro-systems of
loc.cit.
seems slightly different as it is constructed using flat modules over explicit DGAs..

Corollary 2.31
.

The pullback
(\eta_{A}^{*})_{\mathrm{DAlg}}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}({\mathrm{Spec}}(A))=\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))
restricts to an on equivalence
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

Proof.

This follows exactly as in the proof of Theorem
2.26
using the conservativity of the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}.

∎

Remark 2.32
.

After the first version of this paper had been prepared, J. Mundinger informed the author that the results of this section bear some similarity to those occuring in
[
GR14
, § 7.1]
.

Remark 2.33
.

It follows from Theorem
2.26
and Corollary
2.31
that if
f\colon\operatorname{Spf}B\to\operatorname{Spf}C
is a morphism of formal schemes over
A
induced where the formal direction is along
J
, induced from a morphism of rings
B\to C
. Then the induced functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}(C))\to\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}B)
can, after identifying the source and target with
\mathrm{Mod}_{C}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
and
\mathrm{Mod}_{B}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
respectively, be identified with sending
M\mapsto M\widehat{\otimes}_{C}B
where
\widehat{\otimes}_{C}
is the
J
-completed tensor product. An obviously similar statement holds for the induced morphism
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(C))\to\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(B))
. Indeed the first can be deduced by the commutativity of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{C}^{{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{B}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{J}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\wedge}_{J}}

and similarly for the second.

Remark 2.34
.

Using Lemma
2.21
, we may define a
J
-adic formal
A
-prestack as follows. Let
X\colon\mathrm{ARing}_{A}\to\mathcal{S}
be a prestack over
A
. Then we say that
X
is a
J
-adic formal
A
-prestack if the structure morphism
X\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
factors through
X\to\operatorname{Spf}(A).

This definition now implies that by Lemma
2.21
, we have

X=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}X\times_{{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)}{\mathrm{Spec}}(A_{n}),

for some tower
A_{i}
satisfying the hypothesis of Lemma
2.21

In particular we have

\mathrm{QCoh}(X)=\lim_{n}\mathrm{QCoh}(X\times_{{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)}{\mathrm{Spec}}(A_{n})),

which heuristically gives a notion of
J
-complete sheaves on
X
.
Indeed if
X
is a stack over
A
-algebras then
X\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
can be thought of as the
J
-completion of
X
and

\mathrm{QCoh}(X\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))=\lim_{n}\mathrm{QCoh}(X\times_{{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)}{\mathrm{Spec}}(A_{n})),

corresponds to pro-systems of complexes

\widehat{\mathcal{F}}=\{\mathcal{F}\otimes_{A}A/\!\!/(f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{r}^{n})\}_{n>0}.

A similar statement, of course, also holds for
\mathrm{DAlg}(X).

This viewpoint will be useful in our in-preparation work
[
MS
]
. See Remark
4.18
.

3.
Graded derived algebras and comonadicity.

3.1.
Preliminaries on graded derived algebras.

In this section we collect some preliminaries on graded derived algebras needed for our geometric classification theorems.

Recollection 3.1
.

(Local systems of spectra .)
Let
R
be an
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring spectrum and let
X
be a space. Then, following
[
ABG18
, § 3]
and
[
Lur14
]
, the
\infty\text{-category}
of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
valued local systems on
X
is defined as
\mathrm{Loc}(X,R):=\mathrm{Fun}(X^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})
9
9

9

Note that as explained in
[
ABG18
, Remark 3.2]
, in the
\infty
-category of space there is a canonical equivalence between functors
(-)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{id}
. In particular
\mathrm{Loc}(X,R)\simeq\mathrm{Loc}(X^{\mathrm{op}},R)
but we stick with the first category as in the applications we are interested in working with decreasing filtrations.
.

For any morphism
f\colon X\to Y
of spaces, there is an induced pullback functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Loc}(Y,R)\to\mathrm{Loc}(X,R)
. As explained in
[
ABG18
, § 5.4]
and
[
Lur14
, Remark 5]
, the
\infty\text{-category}

\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is presentable and admits all small limits and colimits and therefore, by left and right Kan extensions the functor
f^{*}
admits a left adjoint

f_{!}\colon\mathrm{Loc}(X,R)\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Loc}(Y,R)\colon f^{*}

the
proper
pushforward and a right adjoint

f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Loc}(Y,R)\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Loc}(X,R)\colon f_{*}

which is the usual pushforward.

For a space
X
which admits an
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-group like structure and an
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R
, there are two monoidal structures on
\mathrm{Loc}(X,R)
which we now recall.

Recollection 3.2
.

(Pointwise monoidal structure on local systems of spectra.)
The
pointwise
monoidal two local systems
{\mathcal{L}}_{1},{\mathcal{L}}_{2}\colon X\to\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
to the local system
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\otimes_{X}{\mathcal{L}}_{2}:=(\Delta_{X})^{*}({\mathcal{L}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{L}}_{2})
where
\Delta_{X}\colon X\to X\times X
is the diagonal and
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
is the local system
p_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\otimes p_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{2}\in\mathrm{Loc}(X\times X,R).
Explicitly it’s value on
x\in X
is
({\mathcal{L}}_{1}\otimes_{X}{\mathcal{L}}_{2})(x)={\mathcal{L}}_{1}(x)\otimes_{R}{\mathcal{L}}_{2}(x).

With respect to this monoidal structure, for a morphism of space
f\colon X\to Y
, the induced functors
f_{!}\dashv f^{*}\dashv f_{*}
of Recollection
3.1
have the following properties:

(1)

f^{*}
is symmetric monoidal.

(2)

f_{!}
being a right adjoint to a symmetric monoidal functor is op-lax monoidal.

(3)

f_{*}
being a left adjoint to a symmetric monoidal functor is lax monoidal.

(4)

f_{!}
and
f^{*}
satisfy a projection formula
f_{!}(f^{*}(-)\otimes_{X}(-))\simeq(-)\otimes_{Y}f_{!}(-).

See
[
ABG18
, Proposition 6.8]
for a proof.

Recollection 3.3
.

(Day convolution on local systems of spectra over commutative monoids in spaces.)
Now let
X
be an
E_{1}
-grouplike object in space as in
[
Lur17
, Definition 5.2.6]
. The multiplication
m\colon X\times X\to X
endows
X
with a monoidal structure which is symmetric monoidal when
X
is
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
. It follows from
[
ABG18
, Corollary 6.13]
that
\mathrm{Loc}(X,R)
is now endowed with a symmetric monoidal structure given by Day convolution
[
Lur17
, § 2.2.6]
with respect to which for any morphism of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-grouplike objects in space
f\colon X\to Y
, then
f_{!}
is symmetric monoidal.

Remark 3.4
.

Let
R
now be an animated ring. Then the notion of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
-valued local systems of Recollection
3.1
depend only on the underlying
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R^{\circ}
of
1.6.5
.

Notation 3.5
.

We can now prove the first result that we want. We set
\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}}
be the discrete grouplike
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
space obtained by thinking of
\mathbf{Z}
as a discrete space.
Then
\mathrm{Loc}(\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})\simeq\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
and the symmetric monoidal product induced in Recollection
3.3
induces the usual Day convolution monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}.

Recollection 3.6
.

Recall following
[
Rak26
, Definition 4.2.1]
we recall that a morphism of derived algebraic contexts
\mathcal{C}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
is a colimit preserving right
t
-exact symmetric monoidal functor
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to{\mathcal{D}}
so that
F(\mathcal{C}^{0})\subset{\mathcal{D}}^{0}.
Note that since
\mathbf{C}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
are both presentable
F
admits a right adjoint
G.

As explained in
[
Rak26
, Remark 4.2.25]
these functors induce a colimit morphism
F^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(C)\to\mathrm{DAlg}(D)
which admits a right adjoint (due to the adjoint functor theorem, accessible by the presentability of the source and target)
G^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(D)\to\mathrm{DAlg}(C)
so that the following diagrams commute:

It is now very useful to recall the comonadic version of Barr-Beck-Lurie
[
Lur17
, Theorem 4.7.0.3]
. Our statement follows the somewhat streamlined presentation in
[
Mat16
, Theorem 3.3]
.

Theorem 3.7

(Comonadic Barr-Beck-Lurie)
.

Let
F\colon\mathcal{C}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{D}}\colon G
be a pair of adjoint functors between
\infty\text{-categories}
. Then
F
is comonadic if and only if

(1)

F
is conservative.

(2)

Given a cosimplicial object
X^{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}
such that
F(X^{\bullet})
is a split cosimplicial object, then
\mathrm{Tot}(X^{\bullet})
exists in
\mathcal{C}
and
F(\mathrm{Tot}(X^{\bullet}))\to\mathrm{Tot}(F(X^{\bullet}))
is an equivalence.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to{\mathcal{D}}
be a morphism of derived algebraic contexts (which automatically admits a right adjoing
G
) such that
F
is comonadic. Then the induced functor
F^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{DAlg}(D)
(which automatically admits a right adjoint
G^{\prime}
) is comonadic.

Proof.

We need to check the conditions in Theorem
3.7
. The first condition can be checked after applying
U_{D}
in the first diagram in Recollection
3.6
in which case it follows from the conservativity of
F\circ U_{C}
.
The second fact can also be checked after applying
U_{D}
as the functors
U_{C}
and
U_{D}
preserve all limits.
∎

Proposition 3.9
.

Let
\pi\colon\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}}\to{*}
be the canonical projection and let
The functor
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Loc}(\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}},\mathbf{Z})\simeq\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{Loc}({*},\mathbf{Z})\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
be the induced symmetric monoidal functor of Recollection
3.3
. Then
\pi_{!}
is a morphism of derived algebraic contexts in the sense of Recollection
3.6
.

Proof.

First note that
\pi_{!}
is colimit preserving. Indeed it is a left adjoint and so preserves all colimits. In fact one can write down a formula for
\pi_{!}
evaluated at
E
for any
E\in\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
as
\pi_{!}(E)=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}E(i).
Indeed this follows from the definition of
\pi_{!}
as a left Kan extension over a discrete space.

The formula then shows that for the neutral
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
, the functor
\pi_{!}
is
t
-exact and hence right
t
-exact.

It remains to verify compact generation. The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
is projectively generated by
\mathbf{Z}\in(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})^{\heartsuit}
by
[
Lur17
, Corollary 7.1.4.15.]
. Thus as explained in
[
Rak26
, Construction 4.3.4]
we may generate
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
by finite coproducts of
\mathbf{Z}(n)
where
(n)
denotes
\mathbf{Z}
in grading degree
n.
Since these also generate
(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{\geq 0}
after applying
\pi_{!}
as we see from the formula above, we conclude.
∎

Remark 3.10
.

As remarked in Remark
3.4
, when
R
is animated one can also contemplate
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
and
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
through the lens of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
valued local systems on
\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}}
and
{*}
respectively. In this case the proof of Proposition
3.15
works formally over any animated (in fact
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
) ring
R
. Indeed this is already observed in
[
Mou21
, Remark 5.2]
. We will use this fact in the sequel.

Corollary 3.11
.

The functor
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
lifts to a morphism of derived algebras
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
where
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}:=\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}).

Proof.

This follows from functoriality of derived algebraic contexts and Proposition
3.9
.
∎

Remark 3.12
.

(Naturality of
\pi_{!}
.) As explained in Recollection
3.6
the functor
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
preserves all small colimits and admits a right adjoint
\pi^{*}
so that the analogue of the diagrams in
3.6
commute.

(3.1)

Notation 3.13
.

Let
\mathbf{S}
be the sphere spectrum. Then let
\mathbf{S}[\mathbf{Z}]:=\Sigma_{+}^{\infty}\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}}
. The functor
\Sigma_{+}^{\infty}\colon\mathcal{S}\to\mathrm{Sp}
is symmetric monoidal and when evaluated in the discrete space
\mathbf{Z}
, gives a biaglebra in
\mathrm{Sp}
. If
R
is any
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring spectrum then
R[\mathbf{Z}]:=R\otimes\mathbf{S}[\mathbf{Z}]
and in particular if
R
is discrete then the latter is just the bicommutative Hopf algebra
R[t,t^{-1}]
which is the Hopf algebra of functions on
\mathbf{G}_{m}.
10
10

10

We clarify that the point of recalling this notation here is to show that Moulinos’s argument for
\mathbf{S}[\mathbf{Z}]
goes through for
R[\mathbf{Z}]
as well by base change.

We now recall one of the main result of
[
Mou21
, Proposition 4.2]
. Before this we recall how to describe equivalences of monads.

Remark 3.14

(How to detect equivalences of monads?)
.

Given an
\infty\text{-category}

\mathcal{C}
a monad
T
on
\mathcal{C}
is formally a
\mathbf{E}_{1}
algebra object of
\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{C}).
Let
T\to S
be a morphism of
\mathbf{E}_{1}
-algebra objects in
\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{C}).
To show that such a morphism is an equivalence we use that fact that the map
\mathrm{Alg}(\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{C}))\to\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{C})
is conservative. In other words it suffices to verify that the morphism of endofunctors is an equivalence, and the latter can be detected on objects of
\mathcal{C}.
An equivalent statement applies in the case of comonads. We will use this without comment in the sequel.

Proposition 3.15

(Moulinos)
.

The functor
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
is comonadic and identifies the source as
\mathrm{coMod}_{\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}]}(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})

Proof.

We sketch Moulinos’s proof.
This is an application of the comonadic Bar-Beck-Lurie, recalled for example in
[
Mou21
, Theorem 4.4]
.
The comondadicity is simple to check using the explicit formula for
\pi_{!}
described in the proof of Lemma
3.9
.
11
11

11

We will recall the argument in the proof of Proposition
3.29
.

It remains to identify the comonad
\pi_{!}\circ\pi^{*}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
with a coaction of
\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}]
. One sees first that by point
(2)
of Recollection
3.2
the functor
\pi_{!}
is op-lax monoidal for the pointwise monoidal structure and so
\pi_{!}(1_{\mathbf{Z}})
is a Hopf algebra in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
. Since
1_{\mathbf{Z}}=\pi^{*}\mathbf{Z}
one sees that
\pi_{!}(\pi^{*}\mathbf{Z})=\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}]
.
Then by the projection formula in point
(3)
of Recollection
3.2
one sees that for any
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
one has
\pi_{!}(\pi^{*}(M))=\pi_{!}(\pi^{*}(M)\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}}}1_{\mathbf{Z}})=M\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\pi_{!}(1_{\mathbf{Z}})=M\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}]
and the diagrams witnessing the
\pi_{!}\circ\pi^{*}
-comodule structure on
M
amount to showing that
M
admits a coaction of
\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}].

∎

Corollary 3.16
.

The functor
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
of Corollary
3.11
is comonadic and exhibits the source as the
\mathrm{coMod}_{\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}]}(\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}).

Proof.

In view of Proposition
3.15
, Lemma
3.9
, the first part of the statement follows from Lemma
3.8
. It remains to identify the comonad
\pi_{!}\circ\pi^{*}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
with a coaction of
\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}].
First note that
\pi_{!}(1_{\mathbf{Z}})=\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}]
and this is a Hopf algebra object of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
. So one can contemplate the category
\mathrm{coMod}_{\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}]}(\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}).
It suffices to now identify the functor
\pi_{!}\circ\pi(-)\simeq(-)\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}].

To see this note that from the diagram in Remark
3.12
we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{!}}

So for
T\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
we have

U(\pi^{*}\pi_{!}(T))\simeq\pi^{*}\pi_{!}U(T)=U(T)\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}]=U(T)\otimes_{U(R)}U(\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}])=U(T\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}])

wherein we used that
\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}]
is a Hopf algebra object of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
and
U
is symmetric monoidal and so we conclude by the conservativity of
U.

∎

Remark 3.17
.

It is not hard to generalize the previous constructions to the following. Let
M
be a discrete abelian group. Then one may consider the group algebra
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}M^{\mathrm{ds}}
and therefore the base change
\mathbf{Z}[M]=\mathbf{Z}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}M^{\mathrm{ds}}
. This is the commutative bicommutative Hopf algebra of functions on the diagonalizable group scheme
\mathbf{D}(M)

[
Tat97
, § 2.6]
. Now define
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}^{M}_{\mathbf{Z}}=\mathrm{Fun}(M^{\mathrm{ds}},\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})
and
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}^{M}_{\mathbf{Z}}=\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}^{M}_{\mathbf{Z}})
. Then write
\pi^{M}\colon M^{\mathrm{ds}}\to*
for the canonical projection and
\pi_{!}^{M}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{M}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
for the induced compactly supported cohomology functor of Recollection
3.1
. Then the direct analogue of Corollary
3.16
remains true. More precisely,

(1)

The functor
\pi_{!}^{M}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{M}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
is comonadic and exhibits the source as the
\infty\text{-category}
of comodules over bicommutative Hopf algebra
\mathbf{Z}[M]
.

(2)

The morphism of derived algebraic contexts
\pi_{!}^{M}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{M}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
is comonadic and exhibits the source as the
\infty\text{-category}
of comodules in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
over the bicommutative Hopf algebra
\mathbf{Z}[M].

Indeed this follows directly from replicating the reasoning already present in this section
with only corresponding notational changes.

3.2.
Generalisation to graded algebras over animated rings.

We are now interested in showing that most of the results in the previous subsection work over an arbitrary animated ring
A
. Since
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is not a context unless
A
is discrete we have to be a bit careful about how we approach this.

Remark 3.18
.

Recall from
[
Rak26
, Remark 4.3.3]
that the
\infty\text{-category}

\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}:=\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}}(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{\geq 0}

agrees with animated commutative rings. A similar observation shows that

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}:=\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{\geq 0}

where the
t
-structure is the neutral one, can
by definition
be treated as graded animated rings.

Remark 3.19
.

Observe that by
[
Rak26
, Remark 4.3.6]
there is a natural functor
\mathrm{ins}^{0}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
viewing a derived algebra in graded degree
0
. This functor has a right adjoint
\mathrm{ev}^{0}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
, evaluating the
0
th graded piece of a derived algebra so that
\mathrm{ev}^{0}\mathrm{ins}^{0}\simeq\mathrm{id}.

In particular
\mathrm{ins}^{0}
is fully faithful. Note that after the identification of animated rings with
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{cn}}
of Remark
3.18
the functor
\mathrm{ins}^{0}
sends
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{cn}}
and so we may thus regard an animated ring
A
as a graded animated ring in
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}.

Construction 3.20
.

Let
A
be an animated ring.
We explain why the induced functor
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
can be lifted to a functor
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
so that the analogues of the diagram in Remark
3.12
commute.

Note that the functor
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
satisfies
\pi_{!}\circ\mathrm{ins}^{0}=\mathrm{id}
by examining the formula explained in the proof of Proposition
3.15
. Now let
A
be an animated ring considered as a graded animated ring by Remark
3.19
. Then we obtain a functor

\pi_{!}\colon(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{A/}\to(\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{A/}.

After identifying the source and target with
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
and
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
by point
(b)
of
[
Rak26
, Notation 4.2.28]
we obtain the desired lift

\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\colon\pi^{*}.

Note that
\pi_{!}
preserves all colimits since colimits of non-empty diagrams are computed in the underlying category
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
and
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
respectively, where they can be computed by the proof of Corollary
3.16
and the initial objects are preserved by fiat. Since both the source and target are presentable
\infty\text{-categories}
, it follows that there is right adjoint
R\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
which by generalities on
\infty\text{-categories}
12
12

12

Let
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to{\mathcal{D}}
be a functor between presentable
\infty\text{-categories}
which preserves all colimits and therefore admits a right adjoint
G
. Then
F\colon C_{c/}\to D_{F(c)/}
also preserves all colimits and its adjoint is heuristically given by sending
(F(c)\to d)\mapsto(c\xrightarrow{\eta_{c}}GF(c)\to G(d))
where
\eta_{c}
is the unit. This is
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.2.5.1]
for the case of overcategories.
is given by the restriction of
\pi^{*}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
to the under-categories.
Thus we have an adjunction

\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\colon\pi^{*}

Construction 3.21
.

We have observed in Remark
3.10
that if
A
is an animated ring, then there is a comonadic adjunction

\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\colon\pi^{*}

such that
\pi_{!}\circ\pi\simeq(-)\otimes_{A}A[\mathbf{Z}]
as comonads. Now it is clear from the construction of the adjunction in Construction
3.20
that the following diagrams commute

(3.2)

Proposition 3.22
.

Let
A
be an animated ring. Then the functor
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
of Construction
3.20
is comonadic and exhibits the source as
\mathrm{coMod}_{A[\mathbf{Z}]}(\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}).

Proof.

In light of Construction
3.21
, this follows
mutatis mutandis
as in the proof of Corollary
3.16
.
∎

Remark 3.23
.

Continuing with the thread (and corresponding notation) of Remark
3.17
, we see that analogues of Proposition
3.22
remains true with
the same proof
giving us the following result

Let
A
be an animated ring, then the functor
\pi_{!}^{M}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}^{M}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
constructed analogously to Construction
3.20
is comonadic with right adjoint
\pi^{*,M}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}^{M}_{A}
, and exhibits the source as comodules over the bicommutative Hopf algebra
A[M]=A\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{Z}[M]
i.e.
\mathrm{coMod}_{A[M]}(\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}).

One may think of
A[M]
as the corresponding derived ring of functions on the diagonalizable group scheme
\mathbf{D}(M)\times{\mathrm{Spec}}(A).

3.3.
Continuous comonadicity.

Our aim now is to prove a continuous analogue of Proposition
3.22
in the
J
-complete setting of §
2
. for the reader’s convenience we recall the notation therein.

Notation 3.24
.

Fix an animated ring
A
and a finitely generated ideal
J\subset\pi_{0}(A).

Our first goal is to set up a variant of Definition
2.3
for graded
A
-modules.

Recollection 3.25
.

We define the
\infty
-category of
J
-complete graded
A
-modules as the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
so that the
\mathrm{gr}_{i}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
factor through
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}.
Equivalently, one has

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}=\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}},\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}).

This makes it compatible with, for example, Notation
6.1.1
and Notation
\text{D}.12
of
[
BL22a
]
.

Definition 3.26
.

Let
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
denote full subcategory of
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}
so that the forgetful functor
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
factors over
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

Remark 3.27
.

It is not too hard to give a pullback definition of
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
as in Lemma
2.4
. We do not do so here. However we remark that the forgetful functor
U_{J}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{GrMod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is easily seen to be conservative and limit preserving. The conservativity follows by definition and the preservation of limits follows by noting that the graded analogue of the pullback definition of Lemma
2.4
takes place in
\Pr^{R}
whence the forgetful functor is a right adjoint.

Construction 3.28
.

We consider the functor
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}
as the composite
(-)^{\wedge}_{J}\circ\pi_{!}
. It is clear that this functor preserves colimits as it is the composite of the inclusion

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\xrightarrow{\pi_{!}}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\xrightarrow{(-)^{\wedge}_{J}}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}

and colimits in
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
are computed in
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
and then completed.
One can also note the preservation of colimits by the following chain of equivalences of mapping spaces

\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}((\pi_{!}N)^{\wedge}_{J},M)\simeq\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}(\pi_{!}N,M)\simeq\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}}(N,\pi^{*}M)=\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}(N,\pi^{*}M),

where the first equivalence comes from the fact that completion is left adjoint to the inclusion
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
, the second equivalence comes from the adjunction of Proposition
3.15
, the third equality from the fact that both
N
and
\pi^{*}M
are
J
-complete graded modules, the former by fiat and the latter because
\pi^{*}M
inserts
M
in every graded degree and
M
is already in
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}},
again by fiat.
Thus we have an adjunction

(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\colon\pi^{*}.

Proposition 3.29
.

The adjunction of Construction
3.28
is comonadic and we have an identification of comonads
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\circ\pi^{*}\simeq(-)\widehat{\otimes}_{A}A[\mathbf{Z}].

Proof.

We use the criteria recalled in Theorem
3.7
and follow the ideas in the proof of
[
Mou21
, Proposition 4.2]
to verify them.
We first check conservativity. Note that the composite

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\xrightarrow{\pi_{!}}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}

is conservative. So we need to check that
(-)^{\wedge}_{J}
doesn’t kill anything which is in the image of the composite above. But note that the functor explicitly sends the direct sum
\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}M_{i}
of modules where each
M_{i}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
to their coproduct in
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
. The latter vanishes if and only if each of the
M_{i}
is
0.
This can be seen because for any
i\in\mathbf{Z}
the composite is the identity

M_{i}\to\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}M_{i}\to\prod_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}M_{i}\to M_{i}

and the
M_{i}
are already
J
-complete, so
(-)^{\wedge}_{J}
is the identity.
It remains to show criteria
(2)
in Theorem
3.7
. We want to show that if
X^{\bullet}\in\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is a
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}
split cosimplicial object then
X^{\bullet}
-splits in
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.
To see this first observe that since
\mathrm{GrMod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}=\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}},\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})
, and limits in functor categories are computed levelwise, it suffices to show that the components
X^{\bullet}(i)
for
i\in\mathbf{Z}
split in
\mathrm{GrMod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

Note that
X^{\bullet}(i)
is a retract of
X^{\bullet}
. Indeed there is a functor
\mathrm{ev}_{i}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}
with left adjoint
\mathrm{ins}^{i}
. Note that
\mathrm{ins}^{i}
is also a right adjoint to
\mathrm{ev}^{i}.
Thus we have

\mathrm{ins}^{i}\mathrm{ev}^{i}X^{\bullet}=X(i)\to X^{\bullet}\to\mathrm{ins}^{i}\mathrm{ev}^{i}X^{\bullet}=X(i)

which gives the retraction.

Thus
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}(X^{\bullet}(i))
is a retract of
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}(X^{\bullet}).
Since the latter splits, it follows from
[
Lur17
, Corollary 4.7.2.13]
that the former splits. But
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}(X^{\bullet}(i))
is just
X^{\bullet}(i)\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
, whence we may split the object
X^{\bullet}
by taking the product of
X^{\bullet}(i)
in each degree
i\in\mathbf{Z}
.
Now since any functor preserves split cosimplicial objects (for example,as explained in
[
Mat16
, Example 3.11]
, it follows that the canonical morphism
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}(\mathrm{Tot}(X^{\bullet}))\to\mathrm{Tot}((\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}(X^{\bullet}))
is an equivalence.

Lastly it remains to identify the continuous comonad with tensoring with the completed Hopf algebra
(-)\widehat{\otimes}_{A}A[\mathbf{Z}].
As explained in Remark
3.10
or Construction
3.21
we know that there’s an equivalence of comonads
\pi_{!}\circ\pi^{*}(-)\simeq(-)\otimes_{A}A[\mathbf{Z}]
whence we conclude by completing both the source and the target.
13
13

13

Note that
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}
has the same monidality as
\pi_{!}
, as
(-)^{\wedge}_{J}
is symmetric monoidal.

∎

Construction 3.30
.

In Construction
3.20
we have constructted an adjunction

\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\colon\pi^{*}.

Consider the composite
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\xrightarrow{\pi_{!}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\xrightarrow{(-)^{\wedge}_{J}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
where the second arrow is the completion operation of Construction
2.6
and the target is the
\infty\text{-category}
of Definition
2.3
. For the same reason as the mapping space computation done in Construction
3.28
, it follows that there is an adjunction

(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\colon\pi^{*}

and the following diagrams commute

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

(3.3)

Indeed the commutativity of the first diagram is a consequence of the commutativity of the first diagram in Construction
3.21
along with the left adjointability diagram of Corollary
2.9
, while the commutativity of the second diagram is a consequence of the fact that
\pi^{*}
sends complete objects to complete objects.

Corollary 3.31
.

The adjunction of Construction
3.30
is comonadic and there is an isomorphism of comonads
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\circ\pi^{*}(-)\simeq(-)\widehat{\otimes}_{A}(A[\mathbf{Z}])^{\wedge}_{J}.

Proof.

In light of the commutativity of the diagrams in Construction
3.30
, this follows from Proposition
3.29
in the same way as Corollary
3.16
followed from Proposition
3.15
.
∎

Remark 3.32
.

Continuing with the ideas of Remarks
3.17
and
3.23
, we may define for any commutative abelian group
M
, the
\infty\text{-category}

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{M,{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
as the
\infty\text{-category}

\mathrm{Fun}(M^{\mathrm{ds}},\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})
which is a direct analogue of Definition
3.26
.
Repeating the reasoning employed in this section, we may also get a comonadic adjunction

(\pi_{!}^{M})^{\wedge}_{J}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{M,{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{M,{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}\colon\pi^{*,M}

identifying the continuous comonad

(\pi_{!}^{M})\circ\pi^{*,M}(-)\simeq(-)\widehat{\otimes}_{A}A[M].

We may now define
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{M,{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
exactly as in Definition
3.26
.
Thus we have a comonadic adjunction

(\pi_{!}^{M})^{\wedge}_{J}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{M,{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}\leftrightarrows\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{M,{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}\colon\pi^{*,M}

identifying the continuous comonad

(\pi_{!}^{M})\circ\pi^{*,M}(-)\simeq(-)\widehat{\otimes}_{A}A[M].

Here we view the completed bicommutative Hopf algebra
A[M]^{\wedge}_{J}
as the ring of functions on the formal group scheme
\mathbf{D}(M)\times\operatorname{Spf}(A).

4.
Derived algebras on geometric stacks.

In this section our objective is to study the functor
\mathrm{DAlg}(-)
on geometric stacks. Here we use the term geometric somewhat vaguely to mean roughly a stack which admits an faithfully flat representable affine morphism from a representable (formal) stack, but will specialize in the course of this section.

We begin by establishing flat descent for derived algebras.

4.1.
Flat descent for derived algebras

In this section we show that the functor which sends an animated ring
A\mapsto\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
is a flat sheaf valued in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\Pr^{L}).
This is a relatively simple application of (comonadic) Barr-Beck-Lurie theorem, we chose to prove it since we cannot find an equivalent statement in the literature.

Remark 4.1
.

In
[
MM24
, Corollary 2.19]
, the authors prove that the functor sending an animated ring
A
to the
\infty
-category of
n
-truncated derived
A
-algebras
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A,\leq n}
is a flat hypersheaf. Here
n
-truncations are defined in Construction
2.20
in
loc.cit.

If Postnikov towers of derived algebras over an animated ring converge then flat hyperdescent can be deduced from their result. However, since we did not wish to ruminate on convergence issues, we have decided to record a simpler direct proof of flat descent.

Question 4.2
.

Does the assignment
A\mapsto\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
satisfy hyperdescent for the flat topology on animated rings?

To collect the results we need a consequence of the (comonadic) Barr-Beck-Lurie theorem
[
Lur17
, Theorem 4.7.5.2]
which we will recall below Proposition
4.7
for the reader’s convenience. But first we recall the Beck-Chevalley conditions to define the right adjointability conditions we will need.

Definition 4.3
.

(The Beck-Chevalley condition)
A diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}

commuting up to specified homotopy
\alpha\colon f^{\prime}\circ g\simeq g^{\prime}\circ f
is called
right adjointable
if
f
and
f^{\prime}
admit left adjoints
f_{R}
and
f^{\prime}_{R}
so that the right
Beck-Chevalley transformation

\beta\colon f^{\prime}_{L}\circ g^{\prime}\to f^{\prime}_{L}\circ g^{\prime}\circ f\circ f_{L}\mathrel{\overset{\alpha}{\simeq}}f^{\prime}_{L}\circ f^{\prime}\circ g\circ f_{L}\to g\circ f_{L}

is an isomorphism.

Example 4.4
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}

be a pushout diagram of animated rings. Since the forgetful functor
\Theta\colon\mathrm{ARing}\to\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathbf{E}_{\infty}}
of
1.6.5
commutes with all small colimits by
[
Lur18
, Proposition 25.1.2.2]
, this is also a pushout diagram in
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-rings. Thus the induced diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{B^{\prime}}}

with the arrows implemented via pullbacks is right adjointable by
[
Lur18
, Lemma D.3.5.6]
. Indeed, the right adjoint is given by the obvious forgetful functor.

Lemma 4.5
.

Consider the pushout diagram of animated rings in Example
4.4
. Then the induced diagram of derived algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{B^{\prime}}}

is right adjointable.

Proof.

This follows immediately from
[
Rak26
, Remark 4.2.29]
but we spell out the details for the convenience of the reader.
For a morphism of animated (in fact, derived) rings
R\to S
, the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{S}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
admits a left adjoint given by
-\otimes_{R}S
. The fact that the Beck-Chevalley conditions of Definition
4.3
are satisfied can be checked after applying the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
which is conservative. Indeed, in the latter case right adjointability of the diagram in Example
4.4
solves the problem.
∎

Our descent statement will take the form of displaying the target
\infty
-category as a limit of cosimplicial
\infty
-categories. For this we recall the comonadic Barr-Beck-Lurie theorem below.

Variant 4.6

(Completed adjointability)
.

Fix a base ring
A
and
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
a finitely generated ideal of
A.
Consider the pushout diagram of animated
A
-algebras given as

\hbox to81.21pt{\vbox to46.99pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 40.60583pt\lower-23.49504pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-40.60583pt}{-19.83531pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Consider the diagram of
\infty\text{-categories}
where the arrows are given by completed base change

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{S}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{R^{\prime}}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{S^{\prime}}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}

is right adjointable.
This follows from Example
4.4
because completion is symmetric monoidal.

Note that as a general remark for any morphism
R\to S
of
A
-algebras, the forgetful functor

\mathrm{Mod}_{S}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}

preserves completions. Indeed a
J
-complete derived
R
module
M
satisfies

M=\lim_{n}M\otimes_{R}R/\!\!/(f^{n}_{1},\ldots,f^{n}_{r})=\lim_{n}M\otimes_{R}R\otimes_{A}A/\!\!/(f^{n}_{1},\ldots,f^{n}_{r})=\lim_{n}M\otimes_{A}A/\!\!/(f^{n}_{1},\ldots,f^{n}_{r})

and the forgetful functor preserves limits.

As a consequence the analogous diagram of completed derived algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{S}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{R^{\prime}}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{S^{\prime}}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}

is right adjointable by the same reasoning as in Lemma
4.5
, applied in the completed case using conservativity of the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
of Proposition
2.16
.

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}\colon\mathrm{N}(\mathbf{\Delta^{+}})\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
be an augmented cosimplicial
\infty
-category. Set
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}^{-1}
and let
G\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{0}
be the obvious functor. Assume that:

(1)

The
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
admits geometric realisations of
G
-split simplicial objects and those geometric realisations are preserved by
G
.

(2)

For every morphism
\alpha\colon[m]\to[n]
in
\mathbf{\Delta}_{+},
the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{m+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{0}}

is right adjointable.

Then the canonical map
\theta\colon\mathcal{C}\to\lim_{n\in\mathbf{\Delta}}\mathcal{C}^{n}
admits a fully faithful right adjoint. If
G
is conservative, then
\theta
is an equivalence.

Proof.

This is the comonadic variant of the statement proven in
[
Lur17
, Corollary 4.7.5.3]
. See
[
Mou21
, Corollary 4.8]
for an explanation.
∎

We will verify that the functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{(-)}\colon\mathrm{ARing}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
satisfies the conditions of Proposition
4.7
when evaluated on Cech nerves of faithfully flat maps of animated rings.

Theorem 4.8
.

The
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
valued functor which sends an animated ring
A\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
satisfies descent for the faithfully flat topology on
\mathrm{ARing}
.

Proof.

We will verify the conditions in Proposition
4.7
. Let
f\colon A\to B
be a morphism of animated rings. Let
B^{\bullet}
be the Čech nerve of
f
. Then we have a base change functor
\mathrm{coaug}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{B^{\bullet}}
sending a derived
A
-algebra
R
to the algebra
R\otimes_{A}B^{\bullet}
giving us a cosimplicial augmented diagram in
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
. The adjointability conditions in Proposition
4.7
follow from Lemma
4.5
. Now it remains to check conditions
(1)
and
(2)
of the aforementioned propositions. But they can be checked by conservativity of the functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
via point
(c)
of
[
Rak26
, Notation 4.2.28]
.

∎

Remark 4.9
.

In fact the functor
A\mapsto\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
is clearly valued in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\Pr^{L})
and the base change functors are symmetric monoidal. Since limits are preserved along the forgetful functors
\mathrm{CAlg}(\Pr^{L})\to\Pr^{L}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
we conclude that the flat descent holds as presentably symmetric monoidal categories.

Remark 4.10
.

Now the result in Theorem
4.8
shows that if
X\to Y
is an effective epimorphism in the
\infty
-topos
\mathrm{Shv}(\mathrm{ARing}_{\mathrm{fpqc}})
so that
Y=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}(\mathrm{Cech}(X\to Y))
, then

\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)=\lim\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{Cech}(X\to Y))

where the right hand side is shorthand for a cosimplicial
\infty
-category obtained by evaluating
\mathrm{DAlg}
on terms of the simplicial object
\mathrm{Cech}(X\to Y)
, where
\mathrm{DAlg}
is evaluated on stacks via right Kan extensions as defined in Construction
2.20
.
The equivalence holds in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\Pr^{L})
again by Remark
4.9
.
A proof of why flat descent for sheaves valued in presentable
\infty
-categories implies the Cech nerve criteria above, can be found in
[
Yay22
, Proposition 3.48.]
after noting that
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
is presentable which can be checked by its explicit presentation as a combinatorial simplicial model category by
[
Lur09
, Proposition 3.1.5.2]
.

We also record a corollary which we won’t use but is useful to know. This is the derived algebra variant of
[
BS22
, Footnote 12]
. This is the promised point
(6)
of Goal
2.2
.

Corollary 4.11
.

Let
A
be an animated ring and
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
a finitely generated ideal. Then the functor
B\mapsto\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{B}
satisfies descent for the
J
-completely flat topology on
\mathrm{ARing}_{A}.

Proof.

Write
\mathrm{Cech}(B\to C)
for the Cech nerve of
B\to C
. We want to show that

\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{B}=\lim\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{\mathrm{Cech}{(A\to B)}}

Fix generators
J=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}).

A map of animated
A
-algebras
\varphi\colon B\to C
is
J
-completely faithfully flat if and only if
\varphi_{1}\colon B/\!\!/(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})\to C/\!\!/(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r})
is faithfully flat as explained in
1.6.12
.

This implies that for each
n\geq 1
the maps
\varphi_{n}\colon B/\!\!/(x_{1}^{n},\ldots,x_{r}^{n})\to C/\!\!/(x_{1}^{n},\ldots,x_{r}^{n})
are faithfully flat by
[
ČS24
, Lemma 5.2.2]
.

Thus by flat descent of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{(-)}
it follows that

\mathrm{DAlg}_{B/\!\!/(x_{1}^{n},\ldots,x_{r}^{n})}=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathrm{Cech}(A\to B)/\!\!/(x_{1}^{n},\ldots,x_{r}^{n})},

Note that by Corollary
2.31
we have
\mathrm{DAlg}_{B}^{J}=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{n}\mathrm{DAlg}_{B/\!\!/(x^{n}_{1},\ldots,x^{n}_{r})}

The induced map is faithfully flat for each
n\geq 0
. But since limits commute with limits, we conclude.

∎

4.2.
Derived algebras along quasi-affine morphisms.

We now begin with the main classical results we need. Our work here has a mostly utilitarian flavour. We will remark on this as we get to the needed results.

Construction 4.12
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of prestacks. Then the functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}(Y)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
is symmetric monoidal and preserves all colimits and therefore admits a lax monoidal right adjoint
f_{*}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}(X)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(Y)
, for example by
[
Lur11b
, Proposition 3.2.1]
.

It follows that there is a factorization of
f_{*}
as

\mathrm{QCoh}(X)\xrightarrow{\tilde{f_{*}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}(\mathrm{QCoh}(Y))\to\mathrm{QCoh}(Y).

(4.1)

On the other hand, the adjunction
f^{*}\dashv f_{*}
induces a monad
f_{*}f^{*}
acting on
\mathrm{QCoh}(Y).
It follows formally that there is also a factorization

\mathrm{QCoh}(X)\xrightarrow{\tilde{\tilde{f_{*}}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{f_{*}f^{*}}(\mathrm{QCoh}(Y))\to\mathrm{QCoh}(Y).

(4.2)

The counit
f^{*}f_{*}\to\mathrm{id}
induces a morphism of monads
-\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to f_{*}f^{*}
whence a forgetful functor

\mathrm{QCoh}(X)\xrightarrow{\tilde{\tilde{f_{*}}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{f_{*}f^{*}}(\mathrm{QCoh}(Y))\xrightarrow{U}\mathrm{Mod}_{f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}(\mathrm{QCoh}(Y))\to\mathrm{QCoh}(Y),

(4.3)

where the composite of
U\circ\tilde{\tilde{f_{*}}}
is
\tilde{f_{*}}
of
4.1
.

Remark 4.13
.

The functor
f_{*}
described above is not very well behaved: for example it does not satisfy a projection formula.
Indeed, one sufficient condition for the morphism
U
of
4.3
to be an equivalence is for the morphism of monads
-\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to f_{*}f^{*}
to be an equivalence.
We next isolate a class of morphisms when the projection formula is satisfied. We follow
[
GR17
, § 3]
.

Definition 4.14
.

[
GR17
, § 3.3]

A derived scheme is called quasi-affine if it is quasi-compact and admits an open immersion into an affine derived scheme.

Definition 4.15
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of prestacks.

(1)

We say that
f
is
schematic
if for any affine derived scheme
{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)\to Y
, the base change
X\times_{Y}{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
is representable by a derived scheme.

(2)

We say that
f
is
quasi-compact
schematic if it is schematic and in addition
X\times_{Y}{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
is a quasi-compact.

(3)

We say that
f
is
quasi-affine
if it is schematic and
X\times_{Y}{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
is a quasi-affine scheme.

We now collect some results from
[
GR17
]
which are direct consequences of Definition above
14
14

14

Note that
[
GR17
]
has a running characteristic
0
hypothesis, as their intended applications are to geometric Langlands in characteristic
0
. However, the categorical statements in Proposition
4.16
do not need any restriction on the base ring.
.

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a quasi-affine morphism of prestacks then

(1)

The morphisms of monads
-\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to f_{*}f^{*}
is an isomorphism,

(2)

The morphism
U
of
4.3
is an isomorphism.

(3)

The morphism
\tilde{\tilde{f_{*}}}
of the factorisation of
4.2
is an equivalence.

(4)

For any prestack
g:Z\to Y
, giving a pullback diagram

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

the induced (rotated) diagram of
\infty\text{-categories}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QCoh}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QCoh}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QCoh}(Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QCoh}(X\times_{Y}Z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime*}}

is right adjointable.

Proof.

(1)

This amounts to the projection formula where this is
[
GR17
, Lemma 3.2.4]
.

(2)

This follows from the previous item.

(3)

This is
[
GR17
, Proposition 3.3.3]
.

(4)

This amounts to showing that the canonical morphism
g^{*}\circ f_{*}\to f^{\prime}_{*}\circ g^{\prime*}
is an isomorphism, where this is
[
GR17
, Proposition 2.2.2.]
.

∎

Remark 4.17
.

Note that the results of Proposition
4.16
are also valid in the
J
-adic formal setting of Remark
2.34
. Indeed the statement of Proposition
4.16
is at the level of prestacks and therefore works as well for
J
-completed formal stacks.

We can now prove the analogs of the above statements for derived algebras. However, before we do so we remark on the limitations of what we are doing and what it would be desirable to do.

Remark 4.18
.

Definition
4.15
works with a notion of
schematic
quasi-affine morphisms. In
[
Lur11b
, Definition 3.1.24]
defines quasi-affine morphisms of
relatively
Deligne-Mumford stacks. In characteristic
0
, where one can work with
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-rings instead of derived rings, this yields a larger class of morphisms even in the derived setting.
Moreover in characteristic
0
, one can deduce from
[
BHL17
, Theorem 2.3]
, a classification of quasi-affine relatively Deligne-Mumford stacks as compact localizations of commutative algebras in quasi-coherent sheaves.
It is natural to wonder whether the theory of derived algebras yields a refinement of these concepts in the setting of derived algebraic geometry.

This is under investigation by the current author in collaboration with J. Mundinger
[
MS
]

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of stacks. Then
f_{*}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}(X)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(Y)
admits a natural enhancement to
f^{\mathrm{DAlg}}_{*}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\to\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)
so that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QCoh}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QCoh}(Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\mathrm{DAlg}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}

Proof.

This is
[
Pet25
, Proposition 2.13]
. We recall their proof for the convenience of the reader. First we prove the claim for the functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}(Y)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(X).
Note that by naturality of the functors
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{n}_{X}\circ f^{*}=f^{*}\circ\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{n}_{X}
, there is an induced colimit preserving functor
f^{*,\mathrm{DAlg}}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)\to\mathrm{DAlg}(X)
. Indeed the functor preserves colimits because it preserves sifted colimits, which can be detected after passing to
\mathrm{QCoh}
and it preserves coproducts, which can be detected after passing to
\mathrm{CAlg}.

On the other hand, since both
\mathrm{DAlg}(X)
and
\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)
are presentable (by presentability of the corresponding quasi-coherent categories and
[
Rak26
, Proposition 4.1.10]
), we see that there is a right adjoint
f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}(X)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(Y).

It remains to check that
U\circ f_{*}\simeq U\circ f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}
. To do so, note that the functors in question are the right adjoints of the corresponding functors
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}\circ f^{*}\simeq f^{*,\mathrm{DAlg}}\circ\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}
.

∎

Notation 4.20
.

To avoid clutter we denote the induced functor

f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\to\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)

by
f_{*}
as in our applications the refinement will be clear from context. When we need, for example in proofs, we will revert to the notation of Lemma
4.19
.

We may now begin to prove the desired results of this section. These results will be analogs of Proposition
4.16
.

Proposition 4.21
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a quasi-affine morphism of prestacks. Then there is an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)_{f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}/}.

Proof.

For clarity, we revert to the notation employed in the proof of Lemma
4.19
i.e. we denote the adjunction on derived algebras as
f^{*,\mathrm{DAlg}}\dashv f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}.

First observe that the induced functor
f^{\mathrm{DAlg}}_{*}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\to\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)
is monadic. Indeed this follows from dual to the reasoning employed in Lemma
3.8
, but we spell it out.

Using the coarse Barr-Beck criteria
[
Lur17
, Theorem 4.7.0.3]
, we need to show that
f^{\mathrm{DAlg}}_{*}
preserves is conservative and preserves geometric realizations.

For conservativity, note that
f_{*}\circ U_{X}=U_{Y}\circ f^{\mathrm{DAlg}}_{*}
is conservative by point
(3)
of Proposition
4.16
and conservativity of
U_{X}
. Since
U_{Y}
is conservative, then so is
f_{*}.

For preservation of geometric realisations, let
X^{\bullet}\in\mathrm{DAlg}(X)
be a simplicial object and
|X^{\bullet}|
be its geometric realisation. We need to compute
f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}(|X^{\bullet}|).
Since the functor
U_{Y}
preserves geometric realisations and is conservative, it suffices to show that
U_{Y}\circ f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}|X^{\bullet}|
commutes with geometric realisations. But
U_{Y}\circ f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}=f_{*}\circ U_{X}
and both of the functors on the right hand side preserve geometric realisations.

Thus we have a canonical identification

\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}f^{*,\mathrm{DAlg}}}(\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)).

Now note that there is an identification of monads

(-)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\simeq f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}f^{*,\mathrm{DAlg}}

. Indeed, note that there is an obvious morphism of monads from the left side to the right side, which can be checked to be an isomorphism using conservativity of
U_{Y}
using item
(1)
of Proposition
4.16
.

Thus we have a further equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{-\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}(\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)).

It remains to show that

\mathrm{Mod}_{-\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}(\mathrm{DAlg}(Y))\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)_{f^{\mathrm{DAlg}}_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}/}.

For this note that given any derived algebra
A\in\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)
there is a functor
\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)_{A/}
given by
-\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}}A
and it has a right adjoint given by the forgetful functor
U\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)_{A/}\to\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)
. It suffices to show that this functor is monadic. Since the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)\to\mathrm{CAlg}(Y)
preserves all limits and colimits, the monadicity statement follows from the analogous one for
\mathrm{CAlg}(Y).

Since the functors are obviously equivalent (even as
\mathbf{E}_{1}
-algebras in
\mathrm{End}(\mathrm{DAlg}(Y))
we conclude.
∎

Remark 4.22
.

We view
4.21
as a primitive derived analog of analog of
[
BHL17
, Theorem 2.3]
. We expect to examine this in more detail in joint work with J. Mundinger
[
MS
]
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}(Y)}

In the notation of the proof of Lemma
4.19
, it suffices to prove that the induced map

g^{*,\mathrm{DAlg}}\circ f_{*}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}\to f_{*}^{\prime,\mathrm{DAlg}}\circ g^{\prime*,\mathrm{DAlg}}

is an equivalence. This may checked at the level of modules where this is item
(4)
of Proposition
4.16
.
∎
