4.3.
Derived algebras and the Rees construction.

In this section our goal is to explain the functorialities of derived algebras under the Rees construction from
[
Sim97
, § 5]
,
[
Bha22
, § 2.2.1]
and
[
Lur15
, § 3.1]
.

We will follow the last reference above (even though it works over the sphere spectrum
\mathbf{S}
) as the author actually doesn’t know a reference which proves the derived statement directly

Remark 4.26
.

(What we mean by the Rees construction).
There seems to be some discrepancy in what gets called the Rees construction in the literature. For example, working over a discrete ring
A
, in
[
Bha22
]
, Bhatt calls the equivalence
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m})
the Rees equivalence. This is fine since over a discrete ring the pushforward of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}}
along the structure morphism
p\colon\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}
is almost by definition identified with the
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-equivariant module
k[t]
with
t
in graded degree
1.
However to do this one has to implicitly fix an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})\simeq\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}.
In our derived setting it is useful to work as canonically as possible and so to keep the last two categories separate until we canonically identify them in the next section.

Therefore, for us, the Rees construction will simply mean an equivalence between filtered modules over a filtered ring and graded modules over its Rees construction (which we will explain in the sequel). The equivalence with stack theory will be explained in the next section.

.

Recollection 4.27

(Lurie’s Rees construction)
.

We recall Lurie’s Rees construction from
[
Lur15
, 3.1]
. We work universally over the sphere spectrum
\mathbf{S}
.

Note that there’s an evident inclusion of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{op}}
15
15

15

The source has the same objects as the target. So the morphism is given by sending objects to themselves and the morphisms to identity morphisms.
which induces a functor
16
16

16

We preemptively apologise to the reader for using the notation
\mathrm{Res}
and
\mathrm{Rees}
together to mean different things.

\mathrm{Res}\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Sp}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Sp}.

The unit
\mathbf{S}\{0\}
gets sent to the graded object
\mathbf{S}[t]
which in degree
n
is given by
\mathbf{S}
if
n\leq 0
and
0
otherwise. More generally a filtered spectrum
X^{\bullet}
is sent to the
\mathbf{S}[t]
-module
\mathrm{Res}(X^{\bullet})
which in graded degree
n
is given by
X^{n}t^{-n}
.

By left Kan extensions, the functor
\mathrm{Res}
has a left adjoint
\mathrm{Split}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Sp}\to\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Sp}
sending a graded spectrum
X^{\bullet}
to the filtered object
\mathrm{Split}(X)^{\bullet}
so that
\mathrm{Split}(X)^{i}=\bigoplus_{j\geq i}X^{j}.
Since
\mathrm{Split}
is clearly symmetric monoidal, it follows that
\mathrm{Res}
is lax monoidal whence preserves commutative algebras.

Identifying the source of
\mathrm{Res}
with
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{S}\{0\}}(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Sp})
there is an induced functor

\mathrm{Rees}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{S}\{0\}}(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{S}[t]}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Sp})

which Lurie shows in
[
Lur15
, Proposition 3.1.6]
is in fact a symmetric monoidal equivalence.

If
R
is any
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring then tensoring the above equivalence with
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
17
17

17

Or in fact any other
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\Pr^{L}_{\mathrm{st}})
.
and using
[
Lur17
, Theorem 4.8.5.16]
, one obtains an equivalence

\mathrm{Rees}\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{R[t]}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R})

(4.4)

The same equivalence holds when
R
is an animated ring and
R[t]
may be identified with the polynomial ring
R[t]=R\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{Z}[t]\simeq R\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\Sigma_{+}^{\infty}(\mathbf{N}).

Remark 4.28
.

In the equivalence of
4.4
one heuristically writes a filtered
R
-modules
M^{\bullet}
as

\mathrm{Rees}(M^{\bullet})=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}M^{i}t^{-i}

and thinks of it as a module over the polynomial ring
R[t].

The equivalence in
4.4
has the following features.

(1)

We have the base change functors
R[t]\to R
obtained by setting
t=0
, or equivalently thinking of
R
in graded degree
0
and one obtains the object
\mathrm{Rees}(M^{\bullet})\otimes_{R[t]}R\in\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
which one thinks of as the associated graded of
M^{\bullet}.

(2)

Along the specialisation
R[t]\to R[t,t^{-1}]
where the source is the graded ring with
R
in all degrees, one obtains the module
\mathrm{Rees}(M^{\bullet})\otimes_{R[t]}R[t,t^{-1}]
which inverts the action by
t
on
\mathrm{Rees}(M^{\bullet})
and one recovers

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}(\mathrm{Rees}(M^{\bullet})\xrightarrow{t}\mathrm{Rees}(M^{\bullet})\xrightarrow{t}\mathrm{Rees}(M^{\bullet})\xrightarrow{t}\ldots)\simeq\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}(\ldots\to M^{i}\to M^{i-1}\to M^{i-2}\to\ldots),

and the right hand side is equivalent to
\mathrm{und}(M^{\bullet})\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, the underlying module of
M^{\bullet}
.

Remark 4.29
.

Note that the specializations above are all implemented by taking the graded tensor product in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R[t]}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R})
. Therefore if one were to apply the above equivalences to a filtered
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring
E^{\bullet}
, then one obtains that the following three a priori modules

(1)

\mathrm{Rees}(E^{\bullet}),

(2)

\mathrm{Rees}(E^{\bullet})\underline{\otimes}_{R[t]}R,
and

(3)

\mathrm{Rees}(E^{\bullet})\underline{\otimes}_{R[t]}R[t,t^{-1}],

all are
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-rings in their respective categories.
A similar remark shows preservation of derived algebra structures.

Our goal now is to show that the Rees construction of Recollection
4.27
and the base changes in Remark
4.28
are appropriately functorial in derived algebras.

Remark 4.30
.

We remark on a light conundrum.
For
n\in\mathbf{Z}
, there are functors
\mathrm{ins}^{n}\colon\mathrm{Sp}\to\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Sp}
sending a spectrum
X
to the filtered spectrum
\mathrm{ins}^{n}(X)
which is
X
in filtered degree
m
for all
m\leq n
and zero otherwise. Note that
\mathbf{S}
projectively generates
\mathrm{Sp}
by
[
Lur17
, Corollary 7.1.4.15]
and
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Sp}
is projectively generated by finite coproducts of
\mathrm{ins}^{n}(\mathbf{S}).

The morphism
\mathrm{gr}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Sp}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Sp}
preserves projective generators. However the morphism
\mathrm{Res}\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Sp}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Sp}
recalled in the first part of Recollection
4.27
doesn’t. Indeed
\mathrm{ins}^{0}\mathbf{S}
gets sent to the graded polynomial ring
\mathbf{S}[t]
which is large (and not compact) inside
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Sp}
. On the other hand, once one passes to modules over
\mathbf{S}[t]
in the target, the projective generators
\mathrm{ins}^{n}\mathbf{S}
become (graded shifted) free over
\mathbf{S}[t]
and indeed are preserved as projective generators. A similar remark holds after base change of
\mathrm{ins}^{n}
along
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
for any
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R.

Lemma 4.31
.

Working over the discrete ring
\mathbf{Z}
the equivalence

\mathrm{Rees}\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})

is an equivalence of derived algebraic context.

Proof.

We need to check the conditions in Recollection
3.6
.
The functor
\mathrm{Rees}\colon\mathrm{FilMod}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
is an equivalence and therefore presrves all colimits.
The functor is evidently
t
-exact for the neutral
t
-structure on the source and target and sends compact generators of
\mathrm{FilMod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
to those of
\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
(other than being an equivalence, the latter can also be seen from Remark
4.30
.
That it is symmetric monoidal is checked in the course of the proof of
[
Lur15
, Proposition 3.1.6]
, whence we conclude.
∎

Lemma 4.32
.

There is a canonical equivalence
\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]}(\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbf{Z}}))\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{\mathbf{Z}[t]/}
induced by restriction of scalars
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]}(\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbf{Z}})\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
.

Proof.

The restriction of scalars
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]}(\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbf{Z}})\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
is tautologically monadic with monad given by the graded tensor product
\mathbf{Z}[t]\underline{\otimes}_{\mathbf{Z}}-
.
It follows that the induced functor
\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]}(\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbf{Z}}))\to\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbf{Z}})
is monadic since the forgetful functor to modules is conservative and preserves all sifted colimits (whence totalisations can be checked on underlying module categories). The monad identifies with
\mathbf{Z}[t]\underline{\otimes}_{\mathbf{Z}}-
, but this is precisely the monad for the adjunction
\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{GrMod}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{\mathbf{Z}[t]/}\to\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}).

∎

Corollary 4.33
.

The functor of Construction
4.27
induces an equivalence
\mathrm{Rees}\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]}

Proof.

In light of Lemma
4.32
, this follows from Lemma
4.31
.
∎

Recollection 4.34
.

Consider the
\infty\text{-category}
of filtered
\mathbf{Z}
-modules
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
with the neutral
t
-structure. Then
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
is a derived algebraic context and one defines filtered derived rings as
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}:=\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})
. The connective filtered derived algebras are defined as

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}:=\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}\times_{\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}}(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{\geq 0}

and, in keeping with Remark
3.18
we dub this
\infty\text{-category}
the
\infty\text{-category}
of filtered animated rings.

Remark 4.35
.

The restrion of the functor
\mathrm{Rees}
of Corollary
4.33

\mathrm{Rees}\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]}
induces an equivalence with
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]}^{\mathrm{cn}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]}
, with the source defined as in Remark
3.18
. Indeed this follows from the
t
-exactness of
\mathrm{Rees}.
In particular why the fully faithful morphism
\mathrm{ins}^{0}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathbf{Z}}\to\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathbf{Z}}
we may view animated rings as filtered rings which under
\mathrm{Rees}
get sent to the graded polynomial ring
A[t].

Remark 4.36
.

The morphism of Corollary
4.33
we observe that for any
T\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
there is an induced equivalence
\mathrm{Rees}\colon(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}})_{T/}\simeq(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]})_{\mathrm{Rees}(T)/}.

Remark 4.37
.

The next lemma is a standard argument which shows up, for example, in the proof of [Proposition 5.1]
[
Mou21
]
, which we writed down for further reference.

Lemma 4.38
.

Let
F\colon\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\to{\mathcal{D}}^{\otimes}
be an equivalence of symmetric monoidal
\infty\text{-categories}
. Let
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})
and
F(A)\in\mathrm{CAlg}(D)
be its image. Then there is a symmetric monoidal equivalence of
\infty
-operads

F\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(C)^{\otimes}\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{F(A)}({\mathcal{D}})^{\otimes}.

Proof.

By
[
Lur17
, Theorem 4.5.3.1.]
there is a coCartesian fibration
p\colon\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{C})^{\otimes}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})\times\mathrm{Fin}_{*}
and similarly for
{\mathcal{D}}.
There is a commuting diagram of coCartesian fibration where both the bottom and top horizontal arrows are equivalences.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{C})^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Mod}({\mathcal{D}})^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})\times\mathrm{Fin}_{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{CAlg}({\mathcal{D}})\times\mathrm{Fin}_{*}}}

Then by taking the fiber above
A\times\mathrm{Fin}_{*}
and
F(A)\times\mathrm{Fin}_{*}
we win.
∎

Remark 4.39
.

Note that for any filtered derived ring
S
in
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
and a derived algebra
T
over
S
i.e.
T\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{S}
we have an equivalence
\mathrm{Mod}_{T}(\mathrm{Mod}_{S}(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}))\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{T}(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}).
Indeed this follows because the statement only depends on the underlying
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-rings where it follows from
[
Lur17
, Corollary 3.4.1.9]
. We will use this remark implicitly in the sequel.

Lemma 4.40
.

Let
A
be an animated ring and let
T^{\bullet}\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
. Then the induced functor
\mathrm{Mod}_{T^{\bullet}}(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{Rees}(T^{\bullet})}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
is a symmetric monoidal equivalence.

Proof.

By definition (see point
(a)
of
[
Rak26
, Notation 4.2.28]
) the statement we want to conclude depends only on the underlying
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-rings of all the displayed rings in the statement in the lemma. Thus we can conclude from Lemma
4.38
.
∎

Construction 4.41
.

In the situation of Remark
4.36
we observe that for any
T\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbf{Z}}
there is a forgetful functor
U\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{T}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{T}(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})
and similarly a forgetful functor
U\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathrm{Rees}(T)}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{Rees}(T)}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]}).
By the equivalences of Remark
4.36
and Lemma
4.40
we have that there is a commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{Rees}(T)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Mod}_{T}(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{Rees}(T)}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}[t]})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}

Remark 4.42
.

The commutation in Construction
4.41
is in fact canonical. Let
\mathcal{C}
be a derived algebraic context. Then there’s a forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
which factors over
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
. These functors are natural with morphisms of contexts
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to{\mathcal{D}}
. Now passing to undercategories we obtain canonical functorial commuting diagrams
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{F(A)}({\mathcal{D}})
and with
\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}({\mathcal{D}})
. Now there’s a functorial morphism
\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{CAlg}_{A}({\mathcal{D}})
to the arrow
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{F(A)}(\mathcal{C})
. Composing the arrows we obtain the diagram in Construction
4.41
.

We now end with discussing how to work with filtered derived
J
-complete algebras. We will be brief as essentially the same notions discussed in the
J
-complete setting §
2
and graded
J
-complete setting
3.3
work. We use the Notation
2.1
so that
A
is an animated ring
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
.

We make the following definition in order to be compatible with
[
BL22a
, Notation D.12]

Recollection 4.43
.

We let
\mathrm{FilMod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathrm{FilMod}_{A}
spanned by filtered modules over
A
so that
\mathrm{ev}^{i}\colon\mathrm{FilMod}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
factors over
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

Definition 4.44
.

We define the
\infty\text{-category}
of filtered derived
J
-complete algebras as the full subcategory
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
of
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
so that the functor
U\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{FilMod}_{A}
factors over
\mathrm{FilMod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.

Remark 4.45
.

We can again prove the relevant properties of
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
as in §
2
using the pullback definition of Lemma
2.4
. The only things we need is that the induced forgetful functor
U^{J}\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\to\mathrm{FilMod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
is conservative and limit preserving. See Remark
3.27
for how to prove this.

Lemma 4.46
.

The functor
\mathrm{Rees}\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{A[t]}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
preserves
J
-complete modules.

Proof.

This is obvious from the definition since a
J
-complete filtered module
M^{\bullet}
gets sent to a graded module
\mathrm{Rees}(M)
whose graded pieces agree with the filtered pieces of
M^{\bullet}
. Whence completeness is preserved.
∎

We next implicitly use Remark
4.35
.

Corollary 4.47
.

The functor
\mathrm{Rees}\colon\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A[t]}
preserves
J
-complete derived algebras.

Proof.

The condition can be checked at the level of
A
-modules. Thus this follows from Lemma
4.46
.
∎

Construction 4.48
.

The following diagram commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A[t]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A[t]}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}

Indeed the commutativity can be checked from Construction
4.41
and Lemma
4.46
and Corollary
4.47
.

4.4.
Geometric classification theorems for derived algebras on formal classifying stacks.

In this section, our goal is to prove some basic classification theorems for derived algebras on geometric stacks.
18
18

18

Theorem
4.51
is stated without proof as
[
LM25
, Remark B.5]
. In any case, we need the compatibility with Moulinos’s result
[
Mou21
, Proposition 4.1]
.

We begin with the case of
B\mathbf{G}_{m}
over arbitrary animated rings.
We first recall
[
Mou21
, Proposition 4.2]
.

Proposition 4.49
.

Let
A
be an animated ring and let
B\mathbf{G}_{m}
be the classifying stack of
\mathbf{G}_{m}={\mathrm{Spec}}(A[\mathbf{Z}])
over
A.
Then there is a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})\simeq\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}.

Proof.

The proof will be recalled in proving Proposition
4.51
.
∎

Remark 4.50
.

Note that Moulinos works over the sphere spectrum
\mathbf{S}
. To deduce the result for animated rings one could go over his arguments over an animated ring, or first base change his result for
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Sp}
along
\mathrm{Sp}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}
observing the equivalence

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Sp}\otimes_{\mathrm{Sp}}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}

and on the left hand side we see that

\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})\otimes_{\mathrm{Sp}}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbf{Z}}=\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})\otimes_{\mathrm{QCoh}({\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{S}))}\mathrm{QCoh}({\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{Z}))\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times_{{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{S})}{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{Z})),

where we used
[
Lur18
, Corollary 9.4.3.8.]
Then for any animated ring
A
over
\mathbf{Z}
, one does the base change again and uses the analogous base change in derived algebraic geometry
[
GR17
, Proposition 3.5.3]
. We will use this sort of reasoning to get canonicity for results proved by Moulinos using Tannaka duality in derived geometric situations where Tannaka duality is no longer available.

Note further that a similar reasoning will not give the same result for
\mathrm{DAlg}.
This is already highlighted in Remark
2.17
.

Proposition 4.51
.

Let
A
be an animated ring and let
B\mathbf{G}_{m}
be the classifying stack of
\mathbf{G}_{m}={\mathrm{Spec}}(A[\mathbf{Z}])
over
A.
Then there is a symmetric monoidal equivalence of
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})\simeq\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}.

Moreover the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}

where the vertical arrows are the obvious forgetful functors and the bottom horizontal arrow is the equivalence of
4.49
.

The commutativity of the diagram is a formal consequence of the method of the proof. So we explain the proof now.

Note that the
{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}
is an effective epimorphism and

in degree

is given by
{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[\mathbf{Z}])\times_{A}\ldots\times_{A}{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[\mathbf{Z}])
where the fiber product is taken

we have

\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})=\mathrm{Tot}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern (4.5)

Our job now is to

augment the cosimplicial diagram on the right hand side of the above diagram
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
.

19

19

Here we note that commutativity of diagrams inside
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
(or any
\infty
-category) is a structure not property.

Following the proof of
[
Mou21
, Theorem 4.1]
we will use the functor
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
in order to do so. Note that in light of Corollary
3.16
, the functor sends a derived
A
algebra
T
to
\pi_{!}T\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
equipped with a coaction of
A[\mathbf{Z}]
. The cosimplicial presentation of the category in
4.5
then shows that in cosimplicial degree
n
sending

T\mapsto(\pi_{!}\circ\pi^{*})^{n}\pi_{!}(T)=A[\mathbf{Z}]\otimes_{A}\pi_{!}(T)

makes the obvious cosimplicial diagram commute since the commutativity amounts to the cobar complex for the monad
\pi_{!}\pi^{*}
on
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}

T\mapsto\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.8484pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.8484pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\pi_{!}T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.8484pt\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.8484pt\raise-1.72218pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.8484pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\ }\pi_{!}T\otimes_{A}A[\mathbf{Z}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.14244pt\raise 3.44444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.14244pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.14244pt\raise-3.44444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 116.14244pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\ }\pi_{!}T\otimes_{A}A[\mathbf{Z}]\otimes_{A}A[\mathbf{Z}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 236.03368pt\raise 5.16663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 236.03368pt\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 236.03368pt\raise-1.72218pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 236.03368pt\raise-5.16663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 236.03368pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cdots}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

It remains to check using Proposition
4.7
that this augmentation is comonadic. In this case it amounts to checking that
\pi_{!}
is conservative, which follows from Corollary
3.16
, checking that if
X^{\bullet}\in\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
is a cosimplicial object so that
\pi_{!}X^{\bullet}
splits in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
then
\mathrm{Tot}(X^{\bullet})
exists and
\pi_{!}(\mathrm{Tot}(X^{\bullet}))=\mathrm{Tot}(\pi_{!}(X^{\bullet}))
which is a consequence again of Corollary
3.16
.

The only thing (for the comonadicity claim) which remains to check is adjointability condition. But this is a consequence of faithfully flat descent established already in Lemma
4.5
.

Lastly we note that symmetric monoidality follows from the same argument as that used in Remark
4.9
.

∎

Remark 4.52

(The underlying derived algebra functor)
.

Recall that for
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
one may define an underlying derived algebra functor
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
, heuristically sending a graded derived algebra
S\in\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
to
\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}S_{i}.
Indeed this is just the functor
\pi_{!}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
of Construction
3.20
.
The proof of Proposition
4.51
then shows that this functor is implemented by the pullback
\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
coming from the effective epimorphism
{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}
.

We next prove a statement which requires the notion of
J
-complete derived algebras developed in §
2
and the continuous comonadicity developed in §
3.3
.

Remark 4.53
.

Before turning our attention to the next proposition, we must morally establish an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))

where
A
is an animated ring
J
is a finitely generated ideal in
\pi_{0}(A)
. First note that applying Remark
2.34
via Lemma
2.21
we have the following equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))=\lim_{n}\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times A_{n}).

Now our definition of
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
in Definition
3.26
sets it up so that using Proposition
4.51
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))

\displaystyle=\lim_{n}\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times A_{n})=\lim_{n}\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{n}}

(4.6)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n}\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}},\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{n}})=\lim_{n}\prod_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{n}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{\mathbf{Z}}(\lim_{n}\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{n}})=\prod_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}.

(4.7)

Here we used that limits commute with limits and in the equality in the third line used our Theorem
2.26
. Of course the key non-formal ingredient is indeed our Theorem
2.26
without which we do not know how to justify the equivalence. For the
p
-adic case see
[
AKN23
, Example A.13]
.

However this method doesn’t make it clear that the equivalence of
4.6
is compatible with the continuous comonadicity results of Proposition
3.29
. Indeed it is important that the equivalence be compatible with the continuous comonad for the verification for derived algebras. However, we leave this to the reader and turn our attention to derived algebras. As the methods are literally identical with
\mathrm{DAlg}
replaced with
\mathrm{QCoh}
, not much is lost.

Remark 4.54
.

Note that the statement in the next proposition i.e. Proposition
4.55
cannot be formally proved in the same method as that of Remark
4.53
. The issue is that graded derived
A
-algebras are not just the functor category
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}},\mathrm{DAlg}_{A})
. Indeed objects of
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
may not have any ring structure on their positive graded pieces while the category
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}},\mathrm{DAlg}_{A})
have a ring structure on all graded pieces.

Proposition 4.55
.

Let
A
be an animated ring and
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
a finitely generated ideal. Then there is a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}A)=\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}

where the target is as in Definition
3.26
. Further the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{J}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{J}}

By Theorem
4.8
and Corollary

we have a totalisation diagram

(4.8)

The arrows in this diagram are given by completed base changes by Remark
2.15
whence we must once again the augment the cosimplicial object on the right over
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}.
We may now use the functor
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}
of Construction
3.30
whence the continuous cobar complex for the continuous comonad of Corollary
3.31
, makes the augmentation coherent. It remains to witness the comonadicity of the augmentation to verify the condition in Proposition
4.7
, but other than the adjointability condition, the rest were checked in the proof of Propositon
3.29
.

The adjointability is now checked Variant
4.6
.
∎

Remark 4.56

(Underlying completed algebra functor)
.

We have the completed analog of Remark
4.52
. Namely, by Construction
3.30
, there is a functor

(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\colon\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}

sending a graded complete
A
-algebra
S
to
\widehat{\bigoplus}_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}S_{i}
. By the proof of Proposition
4.55
, we see that this functor is implemented by (completed) pullback
\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))\to\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))
coming from the effective epimorphism
\operatorname{Spf}(A)\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A).

Remark 4.57

(Analogs of Proposition
4.55
for diagonalizable group schemes.)
.

In this remark we continue the thread of Remarks
3.17
,
3.23
and
3.32
. We will sketch how to prove Proposition
4.55
for diagonalizable group schemes.

Let
M
be a commutative abelian group and
\mathbf{D}(M)
the associated diagonalizable commutative group scheme obtained by the procedure of taking the spectrum of the bicommutative Hopf algebra
\mathbf{Z}[M]

[
Tat97
, § 2.6]
. Let
A
be an animated ring and
J
a finitely generated ideal of
\pi_{0}(A).

Then consider the formal classifying stack
B\mathbf{D}(M)\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
.
There is of course an effective epimorphism

\operatorname{Spf}(A)\to B\mathbf{D}(M)\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)

and the Cech nerve in degree
n
is given by
\operatorname{Spf}(A[M])\times_{\operatorname{Spf}(A)}\ldots\times_{\operatorname{Spf}(A)}\operatorname{Spf}(A[M])
, with the product taken
n
-times and the transition function given by action and coaction maps. We leave it to the interested reader to establish an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}(A)\times B\mathbf{D}(M)))\simeq\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}

and we explain how to do so for
\mathrm{DAlg}
. Note that either an explicit check or an analog of Remark
4.53
works.
As the techniques are exactly the same, this is not much of an omission.

By flat descent of
\mathrm{DAlg}
, Theorem
4.8
and using Remark
4.10
we may write

\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{D}(M)\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))=\mathrm{Tot}(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern \mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{M,{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
on the cosimplicial

via

, the functor explained in Remarks

and

, and using verbatim the reasoning of Proposition

, we may test the hypothesis of Proposition

to establish an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{M,{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A)\times B\mathbf{D}(M))).

4.5.
Geometric classification theorems for derived algebras on formal filtered stacks.

We now turn our attention to the promised classification of derived algebras on filtered (formal) stacks.

We will now explain how to use the constructions in §
4.3
to manufacture filtered stacks (i.e. those stacks over
A
whose structure morphism factors over
\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}
) over
A
from filtered animated algebras over
A.

Construction 4.58

(Manufacturing filtered stacks from filtered animated algebras.)
.

Let
T\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}
i.e. a filtered animated ring as defined in Recollection
4.34
. Then by Remark
4.35
we get a graded animated ring
\mathrm{Rees}(T)\in\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{cn}}_{A[t]}.
After transporting
\mathrm{Rees}(T)
along the comonadic adjunction of Proposition
3.22
we obtain that
\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T)
is a comodule over the bicommutative Hopf algebra
A[t][\mathbf{Z}]
.

Note that the coaction is witnessed by the cobar complex

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 22.83453pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-22.83453pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern (4.9)

Now we may forget along the functors
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A[t]}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
and noting the equivalence of comonads

\pi_{!}\circ\pi^{*}\simeq(-)\otimes_{A[t]}A[t][\mathbf{Z}]\simeq(-)\otimes_{A[t]}A[t]\otimes_{A}A[\mathbf{Z}]\simeq(-)\otimes_{A}A[\mathbf{Z}],

we observe that the cobar complex in Equation
4.9
, after forgetting to
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
is equivalent to

(4.10)

As explained in Proposition
3.9
and Remark
4.35
we observe that
\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T)
is still an animated connective ring (i.e. the operations described in this construction don’t add negative homotopy groups) and all the terms showing up in
4.10
are clearly animated commutative
A
-algebras (noting the bicommutative Hopf algebra structure on
A[\mathbf{Z}]
).

Thus in the
\infty
-topos
\mathrm{Shv}((\mathrm{ARing}_{A})_{\mathrm{fpqc}})
the cobar complex for the coaction of
A[\mathbf{Z}]
on
\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T)
gives us a simplicial diagram of sheaves whose geometric realisation is defined as the quotient stack
{\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Res}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m}
i.e. the colimit of the diagram

{\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Res}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m}:=|\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern Note that this diagram lives over the diagram

B\mathbf{G}_{m}:=|\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern and so one obtains a graded stack
p^{\mathrm{gr}}_{T}\colon{\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Res}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}.

Remark 4.59

()
.

As an example of Construction
4.58
, if
T=A
itself considered as filtered along
\mathrm{ins}^{0}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}
we get a coaction on the polynomial ring
A[t]
. Then the stack constructed in Construction
4.58
agrees with the stack
\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times_{{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{Z})}{\mathrm{Spec}}(A).
That the coaction described in Construction
4.58
is the same as the usual one coming from the Cech nerve of
\mathbf{A}^{1}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}
, as defined intrinsically in spectral algebraic geometry over
\mathbf{S}
, was checked by Moulinos in the course of the proof of
[
Mou21
, Proposition 5.1]
. Therefore, for an animated ring
A
, our definition of
\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}
is via Construction
4.58
.
We denote the induced structure morphism
p\colon\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}
.

We now explain why the stacks of Construction
4.58
in fact deserve the name of
filtered
stacks.

Remark 4.60

(Stakcs factoring over
\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}
are filtered stacks.)
.

Note that in Construction
4.58
there is a canonical morphism
p_{T}^{\mathrm{Fil}}\colon{\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}
which comes from the structure map
{\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T))\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(A[t])
. Thus after passing to stacks we obtain a factorisation of

p^{\mathrm{gr}}_{T}\colon{\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}

via

p\colon\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to B\mathbf{G}_{m},

thus giving us a
filtered
stack.

The next remark explains how to use Proposition
4.49
to identify module categories on filtered stacks.

Remark 4.61
.

We already know that there is a symmetric monoidal equivalence
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})
(for example by the tensor argument in
\mathrm{Pr}^{L}
advocated in Remark
4.50
) or just by going over Moulinos’s argument leading to Proposition
4.49
again. Let
E\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A})
and let
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}))
denote its image under the equivalence. Then it follows for general operadic reasons from Lemma
4.38
that there’s a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\mathrm{Mod}_{E}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})).

If
\mathcal{E}
is of the form
f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
for some quasi-affine morphism
f\colon X\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}
then by Proposition
4.16
, we see that

\mathrm{Mod}_{E}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A})\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}(X).

If
E
was of the form
\mathrm{Rees}(T)
for some
T\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
then using Lemma
4.40
we can conclude that

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{T}\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}(X).

This is the format of the argument we will use next for the graded stacks from Construction
4.58
. In particular for
T\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}
it will suffice to show that the stack
p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}}\colon{\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Res}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}
has the property that under the equivalence of Proposition
4.49
, the image of the algebra
\mathrm{Rees}(T)
and
(p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}})_{*}{\mathcal{O}}
agree.

The next proposition identifies that the underlying derived algebra of
(p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}})_{*}{\mathcal{O}}
is in fact
\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T).
Here, by underlying derived algebra, we will use the notion of Remark
4.52
.

Proposition 4.62
.

Let
T\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}
. Then the equivalence
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})
of Proposition
4.51
sends

\mathrm{Rees}(T)\mapsto(p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}})_{*}{\mathcal{O}}.

Proof.

First note that there is a tautlogical pullback coming from the trivial torsor on
{\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbf{G}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\mathrm{gr}}_{T}}

which gives a diagram on
\mathrm{DAlg}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}({\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T)))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}({\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}({\mathrm{Spec}}(A))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{T})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{T})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{*}}

where we used base change for
\mathrm{DAlg}
as explained in Proposition
4.23
.
By identifying the top left category on the second diagram with
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T)}
and the bottom left one with
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
we see that

(p_{T})_{*}\circ(q_{T})^{*}{\mathcal{O}}=q^{*}(p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}})_{*}{\mathcal{O}}

where
{\mathcal{O}}
is the structure sheaf of
{\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m}.
But the left hand side is just
\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T)
and by Remark
4.52
, this agrees with the underlying algebra of
(p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}})_{*}{\mathcal{O}}.

The action on the former is given by the cobar complex of Construction
4.10
. The action on the latter is given by descent along the cover
{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}.
But an examination of the complex in the proof of Proposition
4.51
shows that these are the same actions. Thus we conclude.

∎

Corollary 4.63
.

Let
T\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}.
Then there is a symmetric monoidal equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{T}\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}({\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T)/\mathbf{G}_{m}).

Proof.

The equivalence in Proposition
4.62
is stronger than what is required to implement Remark
4.61
. Indeed we conclude that
\mathrm{Rees}(T)\mapsto(p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}})_{*}{\mathcal{O}}
as commutative algebras so we can apply the conclusion in Remark
4.61
.
∎

We are now ready to prove classification theorems for derived algebras in quasi-coherent sheaves on stacks which are quasi-affine over
B\mathbf{G}_{m}
.

Remark 4.64
.

Let
f\colon X\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}
be a quasi-affine morphism in the sense of Definition
4.15
.
It follows from Lemma
4.19
and Theorem
4.51
that
f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
is naturally a derived algebra over
B\mathbf{G}_{m}
and so a graded derived algebra over
R
. It follows from Corollary
4.21
that one can understand
\mathrm{DAlg}(X)
as the
\infty\text{-category}

\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})_{f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}/}.

Corollary 4.65
.

Let
T\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}.
There is a symmetric monoidal equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{T}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}({\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T)/\mathbf{G}_{m})

so that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}({\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{QCoh}({\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}

where the bottom equivalence is of Corollary
4.63
and the top one is the diaplayed one in the statement of this claim.

Proof.

The commutativity of the diagram is a formal consequence of the proof.

Note that if
f\colon X\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}
is a quasi-affine morphism, then by Theorem
4.21
it follows that
\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})_{f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}/}.
We also know by Proposition
4.51
that
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})
. Now if
T\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}
then the equivalence in Proposition
4.62
shows that

(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A})_{\mathrm{Rees}(T)/}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})_{(p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}})_{*}{\mathcal{O}}/}

is an equivalence. From the equivalence in Remark
4.36
we learn that

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{T}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m})_{(p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}})_{*}{\mathcal{O}}/}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}({\mathrm{Spec}}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T)/\mathbf{G}_{m})).

∎

Example 4.66
.

Let
A
be an animated ring. Then Corollary
4.65
implies an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}).

Example 4.67
.

Let
k
be a perfect field of characteristic
p
. Let
W(k)
be the ring of Witt vectors of
k
given the
p
-adic filtration. Then we can treat
p^{\bullet}(W(k))\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{W(k)}^{\mathrm{cn}}.
Then Corollary
4.63
implies

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{p^{\bullet}W(k)}=\mathrm{DAlg}(W(k))_{p^{\bullet}W(k)/}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}({\mathrm{Spec}}(W(k)[u,t]/(ut-p)/\mathbf{G}_{m}).

We now explain the continuous analogues of these statement. We are in the setting of Notation
2.1
so that
A
is an animated ring and
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
is a finitely generated ideal.

Notation 4.68
.

We introduce some notation to streamline the sequel

(1)

Let
(\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})^{\mathrm{cn}}:=\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}\times_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}}
be the
\infty\text{-category}
of connective
J
-complete derived
A
-algebras. Similarly we define
(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})^{\mathrm{cn}}
and
(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})^{\mathrm{cn}}
.

(2)

The Rees construction preserves
\mathrm{Rees}\colon(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})^{\mathrm{cn}}\to(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})^{\mathrm{cn}}
. Indeed this follows from the
t
-exactness of Remark
4.35
and Lemma
4.46
.

(3)

The functor
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}
of §
3.3
is right
t
-exact since
\pi_{!}
is
t
-exact and completion
(-)^{\wedge}_{J}
is right
t
-exact (see
[
Lur11c
, Remark 4.2.17]
) and the fact that completion can be computed at the level of modules by Lemma
2.7
). In particular
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\colon(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})^{\mathrm{cn}}\to(\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})^{\mathrm{cn}}
is well defined.

(Manufacturing

.

of Notation

and

of Notation

and so we obtain a structure morphism
p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}}\colon\operatorname{Spf}((\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
. We have engaged in minor abuse of notation here.

Remark 4.70
.

We explain an analogue of Remark
4.61
.
Let
f\colon X\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
be a quasi-affine morphism. Then
X
is automatically formal for the
J
-adic topology (e.g. by Remark
2.34
). We know the symmetric monoidal equivalence

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))

explained in Remark
4.53
. Let
E\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})
and
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))
be its image under the aforementioned equivalence. Then again for operadic reasons of Lemma
4.38
we have an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{Mod}_{E}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{QCoh}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)).

Again if
\mathcal{E}=f_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
for some quasi-affine
f\colon X\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
we obtain an equivalence by Proposition
4.16

\mathrm{Mod}_{E}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}(X),

and if
E=\mathrm{Rees}(T)
for some
T\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{T}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}
we obtain an equivalence

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{T}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}(X).

Remark 4.71

(Stacks factoring over
\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
are filtered formal stacks)
.

An analog of the reasoning in Remark
4.60
, shows that for all the stacks
p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}}\operatorname{Spf}((\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}(\mathrm{Rees}(T)))\to B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
admit a factorization

p_{T}^{\mathrm{Fil}}\colon\operatorname{Spf}((\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}(\mathrm{Rees}(T)))\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)

giving them the structure of
filtered formal stacks.

Proposition 4.72
.

Let
T\in(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A})^{\mathrm{cn}}
. Then the equivalence
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))
of Proposition
4.55
sends

\mathrm{Rees}(T)\mapsto(p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}})_{*}{\mathcal{O}}.

Proof.

This is proved in the same way as Proposition
4.62
. However, for completeness, we explain some of the details as we will omit the same in the proofs of Corollaries
4.73
and
4.74
. The trivial torsor gives a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Spf}((\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\mathrm{Rees}(T))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Spf}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Spf}((\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\mathrm{gr}}_{T}}

The adjointability gives us a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}((\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\mathrm{Rees}(T)))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}((\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DAlg}(B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{T})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{T})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{*}}

Following the reasoning of Proposition
4.62
and using Remark
4.56
we learn that the underlying algebra of
p_{T}^{\mathrm{gr}}{\mathcal{O}}
is indeed
(\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}(\mathrm{Rees}(T)).
Note here that the base changes are automatically completed for the same reason as explained in Example
4.25
.

The action on both can again be checked to be equivalent, using the cobar complexes of Proposition
4.55
and Construction
4.69
respectively.

One may worry that the formal stack
\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
might have a different action coming from the Cech nerve of
\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
than the one in Construction
4.69
, but both these actions are base changed along
\operatorname{Spf}(A)\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
from the decompleted (i.e. usual) action wherein this is checked by Moulinos as explained in Remark
4.59
.
∎

Corollary 4.73
.

Let
T\in(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})^{\mathrm{cn}}
. Then there’s a symmetric monoidal equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{T}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}\simeq\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}((\pi_{!})^{\wedge}_{J}\mathrm{Rees}(T)/\mathbf{G}_{m})

Proof.

This follows
mutatis mutandis
from Proposition
4.72
as Corollary
4.63
followed from Proposition
4.62
.
∎

Corollary 4.74
.

Let
T\in(\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}})^{\mathrm{cn}}

and the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{T}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}(\pi_{!}\mathrm{Rees}(T))/\mathbf{G}_{m})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}

This follows

from the continuous analogues of the statements occuring in the proof there.
∎

Example 4.75
.

Let
(A,I)
be an orientable prism and let
d\in I
be a global trivialisation for the Cartier divisor
I\in\mathrm{Pic}(A)
. Then by Corollary
4.74
, we have an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathrm{Fil}_{d}A}^{(p,d)\text{-comp}}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}(\operatorname{Spf}(A[u,t]/(ut-d)/\mathbf{G}_{m}),

where we used the computation that

\mathrm{Rees}(\mathrm{Fil}_{d}A)\simeq A[u,t]/(ut-d)

as graded algebras.

4.6.
t
-structures for derived algebras on stacks and miscellaneous compatibilities.

In this section, which is perhaps the least satisfactory of all of the sections, we explain some facts about
t
-structures in the formal setting.

Recollection 4.76
.

Recall that a stack
X\colon(\mathrm{ARing})_{\mathrm{fpqc}}\to\mathcal{S}
is geometric if there is a faithfully flat
20
20

20

We warn the reader that faithfully flat here is
stronger
than an effective epimorphism for the flat topology. A representable schematic morphism
X\to Y
of prestacks is faithfully flat if and only if for any affine scheme
{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)\to Y
the base change
X\times_{Y}{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)
is faithfully flat in the sense of derived geometry.
affine
p\colon{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)\to X
for some animated ring
R
. Lurie shows in
[
Lur11b
, Proposition 3.4.17]
that this implies that the canonical
t
-structure on
\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
has the property that
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
is (co)connective if and only if
p^{*}\mathcal{F}
is (co)connective in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. Note that, strictly speaking, Lurie’s proof is given in the context of spectral algebraic geometry while our work is in derived algebraic geometry. However, an examination of the proof shows that
literally
the same proof works in our context.

Remark 4.77
.

The stacks of Construction
4.58
are all geometric stacks in this sense.

Definition 4.78
.

Let
X\colon\mathrm{ARing}\to\mathcal{S}
be any prestack. We define as the fiber product

\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{cn}}(X):=\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\times_{\mathrm{QCoh}(X)}(\mathrm{QCoh}(X))_{\geq 0}

and similarly we define

\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{ccn}}(X):=\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\times_{\mathrm{QCoh}(X)}(\mathrm{QCoh}(X))_{\leq 0}.

Proposition 4.79
.

Let
X
be a geometric stack. Let
p\colon{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)\to X
be a faithfully flat surjective representable morphism from an affine derived scheme exhibiting the geometricity of
X.
Then a derived algebra
{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathrm{DAlg}(X)
is in
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{cn}}(X)
(resp.
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{ccn}}(X)
) if and only if
p^{*}{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
(resp.
p^{*}{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{ccn}}).

Proof.

This follows immediately from Definition
4.78
and Recollection
4.76
.
∎

Remark 4.80
.

In
[
Mou21
, § 6]
, the author proves that there are unique maps
B\mathbf{G}_{m}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}
(the inclusion of the special fiber) and
\mathbf{G}_{m}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}
(the inclusion of the generic fiber) which induce maps corresponding to taking the graded of a filtered object and the underlying object of the filtered object respectively. His proof uses Tannaka duality in the sense of
[
Lur11b
]
and
[
BHL17
]
.
There is no hope of replicating his results naively in the derived setting as the next example shows.

Example 4.81
.

Let
R
and
S
be two
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}
-rings. Then by
[
Lur17
, Proposition 7.1.2.7]
there is an equivalence of mapping spaces

\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Sp})}(R,S)\simeq\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{CAlg}(\Pr^{L}_{\mathrm{st}})}(\mathrm{Mod}^{\otimes}_{R},\mathrm{Mod}_{S}^{\otimes}).

In particular the symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is too little information to remember maps between animated rings. While rectifying this is work in progress
[
MS
]
, we turn to other means to get analogs of Remark
4.80
. This was already hinted at in Remark
4.50
.

Lemma 4.82
.

Let
A
be an animated ring. Then there are canonical maps
21
21

21

We explicitly do not claim any uniqueness upto contractible ambiguity of the maps of stacks.

(1)

j\colon{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)\simeq\mathbf{G}_{m}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
which after the identification of the corresponding quasi-coherent categories with
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
and
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
correspond to sending
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}X\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
to
X(-\infty):=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{Fil}^{i}X
.

(2)

i\colon B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
which after the identification of the corresponding quasi-coherent categories with
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
and
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
correspond to sending
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}X\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
to
\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}\mathrm{Fil}^{i}X
.

Proof.

We will only prove
(1)
and the proof of
(2)
is similar.
We may first work over
\mathbf{Z}
. Then this is clear from
[
Mou21
, § 6]
since for classical stacks Tannaka duality indeed applies.

We thus have a map
j\colon{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{Z})\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}
so that

j^{*}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m})\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{Z})

has the intended effect. In particular the following diagram commutes

We may tensor this with
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
for any animated ring
A
and use
[
GR17
, Proposition 3.5.3]
.
∎

Corollary 4.83
.

Let
A
be an animated ring. Then there are canonical maps

(1)

j\colon{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)\simeq\mathbf{G}_{m}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
which after the identification of the corresponding derived algebra categories with
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
and
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
correspond to sending
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}S\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
to
S(-\infty):=\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{Fil}^{i}S
.

(2)

i\colon B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times{\mathrm{Spec}}(A)
which after the identification of the corresponding derived algebra categories with
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
and
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}
correspond to sending
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}S\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
to
\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}\mathrm{Fil}^{i}S
.

Proof.

We will prove
(1)
as the proof of
(2)
is similar.

By Lemma
4.19
and Lemma
4.82
, we have a commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}({\mathrm{Spec}}(A))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times{\mathrm{Spec}}(A))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}X\mapsto X(-\infty)}

where we have engaged in abuse of notation of Notation
4.20
.

By naturality of both vertical composite arrows i.e. the naturality in Corollary
4.65
, we see that the vertical composites on both sides are the forgetful functors. It follows that one has a unique (upto contractible ambiguity) induced arrow

\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\to\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}

which must be the underlying algebra functor sending
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}S\mapsto\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{Fil}^{i}S.

∎

Now we may turn our attention to the formal story. Therefore we now work only in the setting of notation
2.1
which we recall. Here
A
is an animated ring and
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
is a finitely generated ideal.

Lemma 4.84
.

Let
A
be an animated ring and
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
a finitely generated ideal. Then there are canonical maps

(1)

j\colon\operatorname{Spf}(A)\simeq\mathbf{G}_{m}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
which after the identification of the corresponding quasi-coherent categories with
\mathrm{Mod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}
and
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}
correspond to sending
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}X\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
to
X(-\infty):=(\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{Fil}^{i}X)^{\wedge}_{J}
.

(2)

i\colon B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
which after the identification of the corresponding quasi-coherent categories with
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}
and
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}
correspond to sending
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}X\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
to
\widehat{\bigoplus}_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}\mathrm{Fil}^{i}X
.

Proof.

We will only prove
(1)
as the proof of
(2)
is similar. In fact our proof is similar to that of Lemma
4.82
. Indeed we consider
\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{Z})
and base change this along
\operatorname{Spf}(A)\to{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{Z})
noting that
\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m})
is compactly generated (hence dualizable), we may again conclude by
[
GR17
, Proposition 3.5.3]

∎

Corollary 4.85
.

Let
A
be an animated ring and
J\subset\pi_{0}(A)
a finitely generated ideal. Then there are canonical maps

(1)

j\colon\operatorname{Spf}(A)\simeq\mathbf{G}_{m}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
which after the identification of the corresponding quasi-coherent categories with
\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}
and
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}
correspond to sending
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}X\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
to
X(-\infty):=(\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{Fil}^{i}X)^{\wedge}_{J}
.

(2)

i\colon B\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}\times\operatorname{Spf}(A)
which after the identification of the corresponding quasi-coherent categories with
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}
and
\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{DAlg}^{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}_{A}
correspond to sending
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}X\in\mathrm{Fil}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
to
\widehat{\bigoplus}_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}\mathrm{Fil}^{i}X
.

Proof.

We sketch the proof of
(1)
as
(2)
is proved similarly.
This is proved using Lemma
4.84
and the naturality of Corollary
4.74
as Corollary
4.83
is obtained from Lemma
4.82
.
∎

We end this section with a discussion of some
t
-structure issues in the formal setting.

Remark 4.86
.

Recall from Remark
2.34
that we defined a
J
-adic formal stack as a stack
X\colon\mathrm{ARing}_{\mathrm{fpqc}}\to\mathcal{S}
whose structure morphism
X\to\operatorname{Spf}(A)
. For the moment, we may define a geometric
J
-adic formal stack as a
J
-adic formal stack
X
, so that there is an
A
-algebra
B
and a faithfully flat representable affine morphism
p\colon\operatorname{Spf}(B)\to X.

Unfortunately, this doesn’t imply that a complex
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
is coconnective if and only if
p^{*}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{QCoh}(\operatorname{Spf}(B))=\mathrm{Mod}_{B}^{{J\text{-}\mathrm{comp}}}
is coconnective for the canonical
t
-structure induced from
\mathrm{Mod}_{B}
. Indeed the obstruction can be obtained by an examination of the proof of
[
Lur11b
, Proposition 3.4.17]
. The proof relies on a certain comonad obtained by tensoring with the algebra
C:=B\widehat{\otimes}_{X}B
being
t
-exact (i.e. the comonadic version of
[
Lur11a
, Proposition 6.20]
). In our completed setting the comonad is only right
t
-exact and not left
t
-exact as would be required. Therefore an analog of Proposition
4.79
does not hold.

5.
Forthcoming work.

We will now explain some forthcoming work of the author wherein the theory developed in this note will be utilised.

We start with
[
Sah26a
]
. In that paper, given a bounded prism so that
A/I
satisfies some regularity conditions, like either being regular (example in the case of the Breuil-Kisin prism), or being perfectoid, then there’s a good theory of the
relative
Nygaard filtered prismatization.
Set
\overline{A}:=A/I.

This theory will send a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
X/\overline{A}\mapsto(X/\overline{A})^{\mathrm{Nyg}}
a
p
-adic formal stack living over
\operatorname{Spf}(A[u,t]/(ut-d))/\mathbf{G}_{m}
and has the following features (among others)

(1)

We have an equivalence in
f_{*}^{\mathrm{Nyg}}{\mathcal{O}}_{(X/\overline{A})^{\mathrm{Nyg}}}\simeq\mathrm{Rees}(\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X/A})
as derived algebras where the right hand side before the Rees construction is the relative Nygaard filtered prismatic cohomology of
[
BL22a
]
,

(2)

When
X/\overline{A}
is an affine
\overline{A}
scheme then there’s an equivalence of stacks
(X/\overline{A})^{\mathrm{Nyg}}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Spf}}(\mathrm{Rees}(\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X/A}))
where the formal spectrum of a derived algebra is defined as a relative analogue of
[
MM24
, Construction 1.2]
,

(3)

When one extends the construction above to derived
\overline{A}
schemes, then in the
quasi-lci
case
[
BL22b
]
these stacks remain classical.

The results in
[
Sah26a
]
will then be used to study the syntomification of Frobenius liftable schemes in positive characteristic in
[
Sah26b
]
.

Recasting the theory in terms of delta schemes, we will show the following.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
X/W
be a derived
\delta
-scheme. Let
(X/W)^{\mathrm{dR}.+}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}
be the relative de Rham stack of
(X/W)
. Let
X_{p=0}
denote the mod
p
-reduction of
X/W
and let
(X_{p=0})^{\mathrm{Nyg}}\to k^{\mathrm{Nyg}}
be the Nygaard filtered prismatization of
X_{p=0}.
Then there’s an equivalence of
k^{\mathrm{Nyg}}
stacks

(X_{p=0})^{\mathrm{Nyg}}\simeq(X/W)^{\mathrm{dR},+}\times_{\mathbf{A}^{1}/\mathbf{G}_{m}}k^{\mathrm{Nyg}}.

As a consequence we will describe the stack
(X_{p=0})^{\mathrm{Syn}}
as the colimit of the following diagram of stacks

Theorem 5.2
.

(X_{p=0})^{\mathrm{Syn}}
can be described as the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X/W)^{\mathrm{dR}}\coprod X^{p\text{-dR}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X/W)^{\mathrm{dR},+}\times k^{\mathrm{Nyg}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X/W)^{\mathrm{dR}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X_{p=0})^{\mathrm{Syn}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\mathrm{HT}},j^{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(id,F)}

.

Here
X^{p\text{-dR}}
is the
p
-de Rham stack of
X/W
and is related to recent work of Ogus
[
Ogu24
]
. We will use this observation to describe
\mathrm{QCoh}((X_{p=0})^{\mathrm{Syn}})
in terms of Fontaine-Laffaille theory of
[
Fal89
]
, mirroring results in
[
TVX25
]
and forthcoming work of Madapusi-Mondal
[
MM26
]
.

Appendix A
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}
on prestacks.

The ideas presented in this appendix were essentially communicated to the author by Dmitry Kubrak; all mistakes are solely due to the author.

Recall that for a prestack
X\colon\mathrm{ARing}\to\mathcal{S}
we have defined
\mathrm{DAlg}(X)
by right Kan extension in Construction
2.20
.

For our set theory conventions, see
1.6.16
.

Lemma A.1
.

The canonical functor

U_{X}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(X)

is monadic.

Proof.

The functor
U_{X}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
is induced along a limit which we write out explicitly.

The morphisms
\mathrm{DAlg}(X)=\lim_{{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)\to X}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
has transition functors for any morphism
R\to S
given by
-\otimes_{R}R
by
[
Rak26
, Remark 4.2.29]
so that the following diagrams commute

(A.1)

It follows that
U_{X}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(X)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
is a right adjoint with left adjoint
L_{X}
defined as the limits of the left adjoints
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{R}
as
R
ranges over maps
{\mathrm{Spec}}(R)\to X.

We first check that
U_{X}
is conservative. This is easy as the functor is pointwise conservative. We now want to check that
U_{X}
preserves sifted colimits to check Barr-Beck-Lurie. Note that pullbacks for both categories coming from the diagrams in
A.1
commute with colimits (as they are left adjoints) the commutation of
U_{X}
with sifted colimits can be checked pointwise, where it follows from
[
Rak26
, Notation 4.2.28 (c)]
.

∎

Remark A.2
.

Lemma
A.1
shows that there is a left adjoint
L_{X}\colon\mathrm{QCoh}(X)\to\mathrm{DAlg}(X)
with the property that when
X
is affine, then
U_{X}\circ L_{X}=\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{X}
.
We want to understand this adjoint
L_{X}
more globally as a natural monad
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{X}
acting on
\mathrm{QCoh}(X)
and for this we will use a refined result of Kubrak-Li
[
KL
]

Construction A.3

(Kubrak-Li
[
KL
]
)
.

As explained in Remark
2.19
, we know that the assignment
R\mapsto\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
has an endo-transformation given by the composite
U\circ\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}
in the sense that for any morphism
S\to R
we have

U_{S}(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{S}(M))\otimes_{R}R\simeq U_{R}(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{R}(M\otimes_{R}R)).

Thus by unstraightening
[
Lur09
, Theorem 3.2.0.1]
one gets an endo-transformation, denoted abusively by
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}
, of the coCartesian fibration
\mathrm{Mod}\to\mathrm{ARing}.

Here by an endo-transformation of
\mathrm{Mod}\to\mathrm{ARing}
we mean all endo-transformations which preserve all coCartesian arrows. We denote this
\infty\text{-category}
by
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{ARing}}(\mathrm{Mod})
. The result of Kubrak-Li in their work
[
KL
]
is that
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}
naturally refines to an
\mathbf{E}_{1}
-algebra object in
\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{ARing}}(\mathrm{Mod})
.

Now let
F\colon J\to\mathrm{ARing}
be any diagram of animated rings and let
\mathcal{C}
be the
\infty\text{-category}
given by
\lim_{j\in J}\mathrm{Mod}_{F(j)}
.

Then one has an induced monoidal functor
22
22

22

One may also compute this as the pullback of the coCartesian fibration
\mathrm{Mod}\to\mathrm{ARing}
along the diagram
F\colon J\to\mathrm{ARing}
. Indeed there is a natural action of
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}
on the
\infty\text{-category}
of coCartesian sections of
\mathrm{Mod}\times_{\mathrm{ARing}}J\to J
.

\Theta_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{ARing}}(\mathrm{Mod})\to\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{C}).

Explicitly for any
T\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{ARing}}(\mathrm{Mod})
, the functor
\Theta_{\mathcal{C}}(T)
acts on
\mathcal{C}=\lim_{j\in J}\mathrm{Mod}_{F(j)}
by acting compatibly by
T_{F(j)}
on
\mathrm{Mod}_{F(j)}.
This functor is monoidal because it clearly respects composition.

We denote the image
\Theta_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym})\in\mathrm{Alg}(\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{C}))
of
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}\in\mathrm{Alg}(\mathrm{End}{\mathrm{Mod}})
by
\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}
.
It follows now that we may contemplate the
\infty\text{-category}

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}}(\mathcal{C}).

Note that for each
j\in J
, there is a pullback functor

\eta_{j}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{F(j)}}(\mathrm{Mod}_{F(j)})=\mathrm{DAlg}_{F(j)}.

Set
\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})=\lim_{j\in J}\mathrm{DAlg}_{F(j)}
. Then one gets a natural morphism

\eta^{*}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C}).

(A.2)

When
F:J\to\mathrm{ARing}
is the inclusion of the slice category
\mathrm{DAff}_{X}
i.e. the category of derived affine schemes with a map to
X
, for some prestack
X
, then
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}}(\mathcal{C})
is denoted
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{X}}(\mathrm{QCoh}(X)).
Thus the functor of
A.2
is now denoted by

\eta^{*}_{X}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{X}}(\mathrm{QCoh}(X))\to\mathrm{DAlg}(X).

(A.3)

Proposition A.4
.

Keeping the notation of Construction
A.3
, let
F\colon J\to\mathrm{ARing}
be a diagram of animated rings. Then the morphism

\eta^{*}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})

of
A.2
is an equivalence of
\infty\text{-categories}.

In particular for any prestack
X
, the functor

\eta_{X}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{X}}(\mathrm{QCoh}(X))\to\mathrm{DAlg}(X)

of
A.3
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

First observe that the natural forgetful functor
U^{\mathrm{DAlg}}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
is monadic for the same reason as the proof of Lemma
A.1
.

Let
T_{\mathcal{C}}
denote the induced monad on
\mathcal{C}.
Explicitly, it sends
(X(j))_{j\in J}\in\mathcal{C}
to the object
(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{F(j)}(X(j))_{j\in J}\in\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})
along with its coordinate wise induced monadic structure.

Further note that the map
\eta^{*}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})
is also monadic. Indeed, it is clearly conservative and preserves geometric realisations since geometric realisations in both source and target can be computed after forgetting to
\mathcal{C}.
We do need to check that it is a right adjoint. For this we check that it preserves limits. But this can be checked by forgetting to
\mathcal{C}
and using presentability of both
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}}(\mathcal{C})
and
\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})
(in a larger universe) shows that
\eta^{*}_{\mathcal{C}}
admits a left adjoint.

Now the forgetful functor
U_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
factors as

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}}(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{\eta_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{U_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{DAlg}}}\mathcal{C}

with both functors monadic.
Thus it suffices to show that the monad induced by
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
is the trivial monad. For this it will suffice to show that if
X\in\mathcal{C}
then
\eta_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}(X))
agrees with the free object for the monad
T_{\mathcal{C}}
on
\mathcal{C}
.

For this first note that

\eta_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}(X))=(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{F(j)}X(j))_{j\in J}\in\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C}).

Thus we compute for any
Y:=(Y(j))_{j\in J}\in\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})}(\eta_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{C}}(X)),Y)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathcal{C})}(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{F(j)}X(j))_{j\in J},(Y(j))_{j\in J})

\displaystyle=\lim_{j\in J}\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{F(j)}}(\mathrm{L}\mathrm{Sym}_{F(j)}X(j),Y(j))

\displaystyle=\lim_{j\in J}\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{F(j)}}(X(j),Y(j))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Maps}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y).

∎
